May Agni, the invoker(of the gods), the intelligent, the resplendent, the radiant, the very powerful; he who knows(how to maintain) his rites

### Printed p. 540

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 540.*

undisturbed; who is steady in(his own) place, and is the cherisher of thousands, sit in the station of the invoking priest.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk the seer Gṛtsamada praises Agni, describing him with various epithets.

होता (*hotā*)—the one who invokes the gods, that is, Agni, who brings the gods invited to the sacrifice to the sacrificial altar. The word होतृ (*hotṛ*) is used in the sacrifice in the sense of human hotṛ priests, in the sense of Agni who accomplishes the oblation, that is, performs the homa, and in the sense of Agni who invokes the gods. Here it is said to mean Agni who invokes the gods.

त्वेषः (*tveṣaḥ*)—the grown, that is, Agni blazing up with fuel, wood and the like and burning fiercely. When fire is churned in the two fire-sticks it is very small, and is therefore said to be in the form of a child. The same Agni, when set up in the āhavanīya and other fires and burning large with wood and the like and with oblations of ghee, is said to be grown. The word त्वेषः (*tveṣaḥ*) is formed from the root त्विष् (*tviṣ*) (to shine) and indicates one who shines resplendently.

सुदक्षः (*sudakṣaḥ*)—the word दक्षः (*dakṣaḥ*) is read [text: परित (*parita*), as supplied] among the twenty-eight names of strength such as ओजः (*ojaḥ*) and पाजः (*pājaḥ*) (Ni. 3-9); so सुदक्षः (*sudakṣaḥ*) means one of very great strength.

अदब्धव्रतप्रमतिः (*adabdhavratapramatiḥ*)—अदब्बान्यहिंसितानि व्रतानि कर्माणि यस्याः सादब्धव्रता प्रमतिः प्रकृष्टा बुद्धिर्यस्य स तथोक्तः (*adabbānyahiṃsitāni vratāni karmāṇi yasyāḥ sādabdhavratā pramatiḥ prakṛṣṭā buddhiryasya sa tathoktaḥ*). That is, the rites of Agni, namely the auxiliary acts of the sacrifice such as invoking the gods and carrying the oblation, are unharmed, that is, of a nature that does no harm to anyone. The sense is: Agni, who has the excellent knowledge that the rites he performs are of such a nature. Compound words of more than two members (compound words) are very rare in the Ṛgveda. In this word three members, अदब्ध (*adabdha*) + व्रत (*vrata*) + प्रमति (*pramati*), join to make one compound; such usages are very scarce.

वसिष्ठः (*vasiṣṭhaḥ*)—सर्वस्य वासयितृतमः (*sarvasya vāsayitṛtamaḥ*): the best among those who provide a dwelling-place for all; or one who has an excellent dwelling-place.

सहस्रंभरः (*sahasraṃbharaḥ*)—सहस्रस्यानेकविधस्य भर्ता । विहरणेन नानाविधरूपस्य धारक इत्यर्थः । यद्वा सहस्रस्य सर्वस्य भर्ता (*sahasrasyānekavidhasya bhartā | viharaṇena nānāvidharūpasya dhāraka ityarthaḥ | yadvā sahasrasya sarvasya bhartā*). Here the word सहस्र (*sahasra*) means "many"; Agni [the supplied text ends here, mid-sentence, at the foot of printed p. 540].

### Printed p. 541

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 541.*

[the Special Topics on सहस्रंभरः (*sahasraṃbharaḥ*) continue] ... capable of assuming many kinds of forms at his own wish, or, being the lord of many people, one who bears (supports, nourishes) them: this is the sense. In this matter the commentator has given as an example the śruti sentence of the Vājasaneya (Śukla Yajurveda) सर्वं वै सहस्रं सर्वंभर इत्येव तदाह (*sarvaṃ vai sahasraṃ sarvaṃbhara ityeva tadāha*).

शुचिजिह्वः (*śucijihvaḥ*)—शुचिर्दिप्ता जिह्वा ज्वाला यस्य (*śucirdiptā jihvā jvālā yasya*). Śuci means shining; jihvā means the tongue; one with a shining tongue means one with brightly blazing flames; the sense is that the flames of Agni are called the tongues (jihvā) of Agni.

होतृषदने (*hotṛṣadane*)—होतात्र सीदतीति होतृषदनमुत्तरा वेदी (*hotātra sīdatīti hotṛṣadanamuttarā vedī*). The place in the sacrificial altar where the priest called hotṛ sits is named hotṛṣadana (hotṛ + sadana); that is, the sense is the place called the northern altar (uttaravedi). In this matter the commentator has quoted a sentence of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 1-28).

The first two ṛks of this sūkta are, among the eight ṛks to be recited when the rite called Agni-praṇayana (placing Agni on the northern altar) is performed in the sacrifice, the last two (the 7th and 8th ṛks). This Agni-praṇayana is an important rite performed in the animal sacrifice and the Soma sacrifice; the rite of taking the fire that is in the āhavanīya fire to the northern altar is called Agni-praṇayana. While the adhvaryu performs it, he directs the priest called hotṛ, by means of the praiṣa mantra, to recite the mantras needed at that time. Therefore the details of the rite called Agni-praṇayana are explained here as stated in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa—

**The rite called Agni-praṇayana**

अग्नये प्रणीयमानायानुब्रूहीत्याहाध्वर्युः (*agnaye praṇīyamānāyānubrūhītyāhādhvaryuḥ*).

After the rite called Soma-krayaṇa (the buying of the Soma plants) performed in the sacrifice is finished, the adhvaryu (priest) addresses the hotṛ, in order to perform the rite called Agni-praṇayana, which is carried out to take the fire in the āhavanīya pit to the northern altar, and directs him (by the above praiṣa mantra): "O hotṛ, recite the ṛks prescribed for the Agni-praṇayana rite." Then, of the eight ṛks that the hotṛ has to recite for this purpose, he recites—

(1) प्र देवं देव्या धिया भरता जातवेदसं । (*pra devaṃ devyā dhiyā bharatā jātavedasaṃ |*)
हव्या नो वक्षदानुषगिति गायत्रीं ब्राह्मणस्यानुब्रूयात्॥ (*havyā no vakṣadānuṣagiti gāyatrīṃ brāhmaṇasyānubrūyāt‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-176-2)

the first ṛk—[the main sense of this ṛk: the sacrificer addresses the priests: "O priests, taking this Agni named Jātavedas to the neighbourhood of the northern altar, with the praises and the like fitting for this god and with the rite suited to the time, ...

### Printed p. 542

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 542.*

... let this Agni afterwards carry to the gods the oblations and the like that we offer," he says]. The ṛk stated above is in the Gāyatrī metre. If it is asked why a ṛk of this metre is to be recited—

गायत्रो वै ब्राह्मणस्तेजो वै ब्रह्मवर्चसं गायत्री तेजसैनं तद्ब्रह्म- (*gāyatro vai brāhmaṇastejo vai brahmavarcasaṃ gāyatrī tejasainaṃ tadbrahma-*)
वर्चसेन समर्धयति (*varcasena samardhayati*).

If the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice is a Brahmin, a ṛk of the Gāyatrī metre must be recited, because the Brahmin is of the nature of Gāyatrī. Since the Brahmin was born from the mouth of Prajāpati (the Supreme Brahman) (ब्राह्मणोऽस्य मुखमासीत् (*brāhmaṇo 'sya mukhamāsīt*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-90-12), it is the radiance of Prajāpati that is in the Brahmin. In Gāyatrī too there is such brahma-radiance (brahmavarcas). Therefore the Brahmin must perform this rite by means of the ṛk of the Gāyatrī metre, which is endowed with brahma-radiance. On the question which metre's ṛks the Brahmin, the Kṣatriya and the Vaiśya must recite in sacrificial rites, the sentence of the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa of the Yajurveda runs thus—

गायत्रीभिर्बाह्मणस्यादध्यात् (*gāyatrībhirbāhmaṇasyādadhyāt*) । गायत्रछंदा वै ब्राह्मणः (*gāyatrachaṃdā vai brāhmaṇaḥ*) । स्वस्य छंदसः (*svasya chaṃdasaḥ*)
प्रत्ययनस्त्वाय (*pratyayanastvāya*) । त्रिष्टुग्भी राजन्यस्य (*triṣṭugbhī rājanyasya*) । त्रिष्टुप्छंदा वै राजन्यः (*triṣṭupchaṃdā vai rājanyaḥ*) । स्वस्य (*svasya*)
छंदसः प्रत्ययनस्त्वाय (*chaṃdasaḥ pratyayanastvāya*) । जगतीभिर्वैश्यस्य (*jagatībhirvaiśyasya*) । जगतीछंदा वै वैश्यः (*jagatīchaṃdā vai vaiśyaḥ*) । स्वस्य (*svasya*)
छंदसः प्रत्ययनस्त्वाय (*chaṃdasaḥ pratyayanastvāya*)
(Tai. Brā. 1-1-9-6, 7)

That is, the Brahmin must perform the agnyādhāna (the newly begun sacrificial rite) with mantras of the Gāyatrī metre, because the Brahmin is of the nature of the Gāyatrī metre. For this very reason, to obtain brahma-radiance, the sacrificial rites must be performed using ṛks of the Gāyatrī metre. Likewise it is said that Kṣatriyas must perform sacrificial rites with ṛks of the Triṣṭup metre, and Vaiśyas with ṛks of the Jagatī metre. Because it is so said, if the sacrificer is a Kṣatriya, then in the Agni-praṇayana rite—

इमं महे विदथ्याय शूषमिति त्रिष्टुभं राजन्यस्यानुब्रूयात् (*imaṃ mahe vidathyāya śūṣamiti triṣṭubhaṃ rājanyasyānubrūyāt*) त्रैष्टुभो (*traiṣṭubho*)
वै राजन्य ओजो वा इंद्रियं वीर्यं त्रिष्टुबोजसैवैनं तदिंद्रियेण (*vai rājanya ojo vā iṃdriyaṃ vīryaṃ triṣṭubojasaivainaṃ tadiṃdriyeṇa*)
वीर्येण समर्धयति (*vīryeṇa samardhayati*) ।

The ṛks of the Triṣṭup metre beginning इमं महे विदथ्याय शूषं (*imaṃ mahe vidathyāya śūṣaṃ*) must be recited, because the Kṣatriya was produced from the arm of Prajāpati (बाहू राजन्यः कृतः (*bāhū rājanyaḥ kṛtaḥ*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-90-12). The arm is the symbol of ojas (strength), indriya, and vīrya (strength, valour, power and capacity). Since ojas and the rest are obtained from the Triṣṭup metre, the Kṣatriya must perform the sacrificial rite only with ṛks of the Triṣṭup metre. Therefore the hotṛ—

### Printed p. 543

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 543.*

(1) इमं महे विदथ्याय शूषं शश्वत् कृत्व ईड्याय प्र जभ्रुः । (*imaṃ mahe vidathyāya śūṣaṃ śaśvat kṛtva īḍyāya pra jabhruḥ |*)
शृणोतु नो दम्येभिरनीकैः शृणोत्वग्निर्दिवैरजस्रः ॥ (*śṛṇotu no damyebhiranīkaiḥ śṛṇotvagnirdivairajasraḥ ‖*)
(Ma. [sic as printed] Saṃ. 3-54-1)

recites this ṛk of the Triṣṭup metre. The explanation of the meaning of this ṛk too is stated in the Brāhmaṇa sentence itself—

शश्वत्कृत्व ईड्याय प्रजभ्रुरिति स्वानामेवैनं तच्छृष्ठ्यं गमयति शृणोतु (*śaśvatkṛtva īḍyāya prajabhruriti svānāmevainaṃ tacchṛṣṭhyaṃ gamayati śṛṇotu*)
नो दम्येभिरनीकैः शृणोत्वग्निर्दिव्यैरजस्र इति आजरसं हास्मिन्नजस्रो (*no damyebhiranīkaiḥ śṛṇotvagnirdivyairajasra iti ājarasaṃ hāsminnajasro*)
दीदाय य एवं वेद (*dīdāya ya evaṃ veda*) ॥

The meaning of the above ṛk इमं महे (*imaṃ mahe*) is this: the priests brought to the neighbourhood of the northern altar Agni, the cause of happiness (for the success of the kings' sacrifice). Agni is one who is always praised. The hotṛ brings the sacrificer as the chief (so that he stands in front of his kinsmen). This is the meaning of the first half of this ṛk. The meaning of the second half: to hold the enemy armies of the sacrificer in check, may Agni, helping the sacrificer with his armies of flame, hear our praises and the like. May Agni know the sacrificial rites that the sacrificers perform well, and may this Agni, endowed with enjoyments and the like fit for the world of the gods, remaining in our houses, protect our sacrificers always, that is, throughout their lives: this is the main sense. Now the mantras to be recited by Vaiśyas are as follows—

अयमिह प्रथमो धायि धातृभिरिति जगतीं वैश्यस्यानुब्रूयात् (*ayamiha prathamo dhāyi dhātṛbhiriti jagatīṃ vaiśyasyānubrūyāt*) । जागतो (*jāgato*)
वै वैश्यो जागताः पशवः पशुभिरेवैनं तत्समर्थयति (*vai vaiśyo jāgatāḥ paśavaḥ paśubhirevainaṃ tatsamarthayati*) । वनेषु चित्रं (*vaneṣu citraṃ*)
विभ्वं विशेषिविश इत्यभिरूपा यद्यज्ञेऽभिरूपं तत्समृद्धं (*vibhvaṃ viśeṣiviśa ityabhirūpā yadyajñe 'bhirūpaṃ tatsamṛddhaṃ*) ॥

The Vaiśya was produced from the middle region or the thigh region of Prajāpati (ऊरू तदस्य यद्वैश्यः (*ūrū tadasya yadvaiśyaḥ*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-90-12). This matter is stated in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā: मध्यतः सप्तदशं निरमि- (*madhyataḥ saptadaśaṃ nirami-*)मात तं विश्वे देवा देवता अन्वसृज्यंत जगतीछंदो वैरूपं साम वैश्यो वै मनुष्याणां (*māta taṃ viśve devā devatā anvasṛjyaṃta jagatīchaṃdo vairūpaṃ sāma vaiśyo vai manuṣyāṇāṃ*). The Vaiśya is of the nature of the Jagatī metre, because cattle (livestock wealth) too are of the nature of the Jagatī metre. For him to be endowed with this wealth of cattle, the Vaiśya must recite ṛks of the Jagatī metre and perform the sacrificial rite. Therefore, if the sacrificer is a Vaiśya, the hotṛ—

(1) अयमिह प्रथमो धायि धातृभिर्होता यजिष्ठो अध्वरेष्वीड्यः । (*ayamiha prathamo dhāyi dhātṛbhirhotā yajiṣṭho adhvareṣvīḍyaḥ |*)
यमप्नवानो भृगवो विरुरुचुर्वनेषु चित्रं विभ्वं विशेषिविशे ॥ (*yamapnavāno bhṛgavo virurucurvaneṣu citraṃ vibhvaṃ viśeṣiviśe ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-7-1)

### Printed p. 544

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 544.*

... recites this ṛk of the Jagatī metre. For, as said before, the Vaiśya belongs to the Jagatī metre. Cattle (livestock wealth) also belong to the same class. Therefore the Vaiśya recites a mantra of the Jagatī metre in order to prosper, endowed with wealth. In the last pāda of this ṛk there is a word that indicates the word Vaiśya, namely विशॆविशे (*viśèviśe*) (as supplied). In sacrifices, if ṛks composed of words suited to the occasion are recited, the intended fruit is obtained in a special degree. Therefore the recitation here of this ṛk of the Jagatī metre, which contains a word indicating the Vaiśya, is in keeping with this occasion. In this way, having stated one first mantra (ṛk) each, of three different metres, Gāyatrī, Triṣṭup and Jagatī, according to the fruit desired by the Brahmin, the Kṣatriya and the Vaiśya respectively, the hotṛ now recites the second ṛk (mantra), which is to be recited in common by those of all the varṇas in this Agni-praṇayana rite—

अयमु ष्य प्र देवयुरिति अनुष्टुभि वाचं विसृजते । वाग्वा अनुष्टुब्बाच्येव (*ayamu ṣya pra devayuriti anuṣṭubhi vācaṃ visṛjate | vāgvā anuṣṭubbācyeva*)
तद्वाचं विसृजते । आयमु ष्य इति यदाहायमु स्यागमं या पुरा (*tadvācaṃ visṛjate | āyamu ṣya iti yadāhāyamu syāgamaṃ yā purā*)
गंधर्वेष्टवाक्समित्येव तद्वाक्प्रब्रूते ॥ (*gaṃdharveṣṭavāksamityeva tadvākprabrūte ‖*)

From the time of the Soma-krayaṇa (buying of the Soma), and in the rites performed from then up to now, the mantras were recited in a low voice, that is, in a soft tone, according to the injunction that mantras be recited in the upāṃśu manner. Now, that is, from here onwards, that rule is dismissed; that is, giving up speaking softly, the mantras will be recited in a firm (loud) voice. This procedure is named vācovisarjana (release of speech). It is well known that it is begun with a ṛk of the Anuṣṭup metre, because speech (vāk) itself is of the nature of the Anuṣṭup metre; therefore by a mantra of that metre itself the soft speaking is given up. Thus the hotṛ—

(2) अयमु ष्य प्र देवयुर्होता यज्ञाय नीयते । (*ayamu ṣya pra devayurhotā yajñāya nīyate |*)
रथो न योरभीवृतो फृणीनाइच्छितति त्मना ॥ (*ratho na yorabhīvṛto phṛṇīnāicchitati tmanā ‖*)
(Ma. [sic as printed] Saṃ. 10-176-3)

recites this ṛk in a firm voice. This speech (the goddess of speech) was formerly among the Gandharvas. Now she speaks thus: "I was formerly among the Gandharvas at the time of the Soma-krayaṇa. Now I have come back." Since this is said, the hotṛ recites the mantra in a firm voice accordingly.

On the matter of the goddess of speech having been among the Gandharvas and saying that she has now come back, an episode is told in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (तग्० सोममाह्रियमाणं गंधर्वो विश्वावसुः पर्यमुष्णात्स..................तेभ्य एव ददति ॥ (*tag0 somamāhriyamāṇaṃ gaṃdharvo viśvāvasuḥ paryamuṣṇātsa..................tebhya eva dadati ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-1-6-5, 6) [the supplied text has an odd start तग्० (*tag0*) and a run of dots]). While the Soma that was in the world of heaven was being brought, the Gandharva named Viśvāvasu stole it in the middle of the way and

### Printed p. 545

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 545.*

hid it for three nights. Even now, after the Soma-krayaṇa, it has become the custom to keep it so for three nights. Then the gods all assembled and deliberated how to bring Soma back from that Gandharva, and a thought suddenly struck them: the Gandharvas are greatly enamoured of women. So they thought that they could give a beautiful young woman to the Gandharvas and buy the Soma in exchange. Thereupon the gods called the goddess of speech and said: "O goddess of speech, assume the form of a beautiful young woman; we shall sell you to the Gandharvas, bring the Soma and complete the sacrifice." The goddess of speech agreed. Then they sold that beautiful young woman to the Gandharva king named Viśvāvasu (as supplied) and brought the Soma. After the gods had gone, the goddess of speech, who was in the form of the beautiful young woman, took the form of a red deer and ran from the Gandharvas to the neighbourhood of the gods. It is this matter that is stated here as the goddess of speech saying that she has come back. Everything that went on from the time the goddess of speech, in the form of a beautiful young woman, was with the Gandharvas until she came again to the gods, namely the Soma-krayaṇa (buying of Soma) and the rites so far performed in the Agni-praṇayana, is recited in a low voice (softly) because the goddess of speech is not near. After the goddess of speech has come back, the hotṛ begins fearlessly to recite the mantras in a firm voice: this is the main sense.

Now the hotṛ proceeds and recites the third ṛk.

अयमग्निरुरुष्यतीति अयं वा अग्निरुरुष्यति अमृता दिव जन्मन इत्यमृतत्वमेवास्मिंस्तद्दधाति सहसश्चित्सहीयान्देवो जीवातवे कृत इति देवो (*ayamagniruruṣyatīti ayaṃ vā agniruruṣyati amṛtā diva janmana ityamṛtatvamevāsmiṃstaddadhāti sahasaścitsahīyāndevo jīvātave kṛta iti devo*)
हैष एतज्जीवातवे कृतो यदग्निः ॥ (*haiṣa etajjīvātave kṛto yadagniḥ ‖*)

The mantra that is the third ṛk is as follows—

(3) अयमग्निरुरुष्यत्यमृतादिव जन्मनः । (*ayamagniruruṣyatyamṛtādiva janmanaḥ |*)
सहसश्चित्सहीयान् देवो जीवातवे कृतः ॥ (*sahasaścitsahīyān devo jīvātave kṛtaḥ ‖*)
(Ma. [sic as printed] Saṃ. 18-176-4 [sic, as printed])

The main sense of this ṛk: because this Agni produces for the sacrificers a birth of divine form, he protects them by warding off the fear that comes from men and the fear that comes from the gods. This Agni is exceedingly stronger than strength. He has been made (created) for the sacrifice or for granting long life. Thus here the hotṛ, by reciting the first half of this ṛk, makes the sacrificer free from death, that is, one possessed of long life. By reciting the second half, the power of this Agni becomes very capable. Therefore by reciting this ṛk Agni becomes favourable to protect our life.

*Stray line kept from the supplied text (end of page 545, not part of the sense):* [35] Vol. 14

### Printed p. 546

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 546.*

इळायास्त्वा पदे वयं नाभा पृथिव्या अधीति एतद्वा इळायास्पदं यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः । जातवेदो निधीमहीति निधास्यंतो ह्येनं भवंति । अग्ने (*iḷāyāstvā pade vayaṃ nābhā pṛthivyā adhīti etadvā iḷāyāspadaṃ yaduttaravedīnābhiḥ | jātavedo nidhīmahīti nidhāsyaṃto hyenaṃ bhavaṃti | agne*)
हव्याय वोळ्हव इति हव्यं हि वक्ष्यन्भवति ॥ (*havyāya voḷhava iti havyaṃ hi vakṣyanbhavati ‖*)

Then the hotṛ recites the fourth ṛk (the supplied text prints मुक्कवन्नु (*mukkavannu*) for ऋक्कन्नु (*ṛkkannu*), apparently a misprint)—

(4) इळायास्त्वा पदे वयं नःभा पृथिव्या अधि । (*iḷāyāstvā pade vayaṃ naḥbhā pṛthivyā adhi |*)
जातवेदो नि धीमह्यग्ने हव्याय वोळ्हवे ॥ (*jātavedo ni dhīmahyagne havyāya voḷhave ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-29-4)

The main sense of this ṛk: "O Agni, named Jātavedas, we shall establish (place) you in the navel-place of the earth, the northern altar." The word इळा (*iḷā*) denotes the cow. इळायाः पदे (*iḷāyāḥ pade*) means: the navel region of the northern altar, on which the feet of the cow, made excellent by rites such as the pressing of ghee, are placed, is described by the words इळायास्पदं (*iḷāyāspadaṃ*). By the sentence जातवेदो निधीमहि (*jātavedo nidhīmahi*) the sense is indicated that we priests place Agni on the northern altar. The sentence in the last pāda, अग्ने हव्याय वोळ्तवे (*agne havyāya voḷtave*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā reads वोळ्हवे (*voḷhave*)), indicates the sense that Agni is established here to carry the oblations and the like to the gods.

अग्ने विश्वेभिः स्वनीक देवैरूर्णावंतं प्रथमः सीद योनिमिति विश्वैरेवैनं तद्देवैः सहासादयति । कुलायिनं धृतवंतं सवित्र इति कुलायमिव (*agne viśvebhiḥ svanīka devairūrṇāvaṃtaṃ prathamaḥ sīda yonimiti viśvairevainaṃ taddevaiḥ sahāsādayati | kulāyinaṃ dhṛtavaṃtaṃ savitra iti kulāyamiva*)
ह्येतदज्ञे क्रियते यत्पैतुदारवाः परिधयो गुग्गु लूर्णास्तुकाः सुगंधितेजनानीति यज्ञं नय यजमानाय साध्विति यज्ञमेव तदृजुधा प्रतिष्ठापयति ॥ (*hyetadajñe kriyate yatpaitudāravāḥ paridhayo guggu lūrṇāstukāḥ sugaṃdhitejanānīti yajñaṃ naya yajamānāya sādhviti yajñameva tadṛjudhā pratiṣṭhāpayati ‖*)

After that the hotṛ recites the fifth ṛk—

(5) अग्ने विश्वेभिः स्वनीक देवैरूर्णावंतं प्रथमः सीद योनिं । (*agne viśvebhiḥ svanīka devairūrṇāvaṃtaṃ prathamaḥ sīda yoniṃ |*)
कुलायिनं धृतवंतं सवित्रे यज्ञं नय यजमानाय साधु ॥ (*kulāyinaṃ dhṛtavaṃtaṃ savitre yajñaṃ naya yajamānāya sādhu ‖*)
(Mu. [sic as printed] Saṃ. 6-15-16)

The main sense of the first half of this ṛk: "O Agni, you who are endowed with armies in the form of flames, come, as the chief of all the gods, and sit in this navel-place called the northern altar." By the use of the word विश्वेभिः (*viśvebhiḥ*) here it is indicated that all the gods are established together with Agni; that is, the sense is that Agni, together with all the gods, will sit on the northern altar, which is like a bird's nest, made soft by spreading strands of wool. Being on the northern altar, the navel region, which is abundantly supplied with firewood and the like and sprinkled with ghee, ... the sacrificer's sacrificial rites (text breaks off at the end of this page)

### Printed p. 547

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 547.*

... "perform well" is the sense of the second half. Here the words कुलायिनं धृतवंतं (*kulāyinaṃ dhṛtavaṃtaṃ*) in the third pāda suggest a bird's nest. That is, a bird builds its nest by weaving together small twigs of the khadira or devadāru tree, gum (resin), woollen threads, and a kind of fragrant grass. The navel-place of Agni is compared here to that. How? In building a nest the bird uses twigs; here the logs called paridhi are placed around Agni. Likewise, in place of gum, the fragrant substances such as ghee placed for the homa; in place of woollen threads, sacrificial animals such as the goat; in place of grass, the fuel-sticks (samidh) are prepared and placed around Agni. Thus the northern altar (the fire-pit) established for Agni is described by comparison with a bird's nest. The prayer expressed here is that Agni, established in the northern altar which is like such a bird's nest, may perform well the sacrificial acts of the sacrificer.

सीद होतः स्व उ लोके चिकित्वानित्यग्निर्वै देवानां होता तस्यैष स्वो लोको यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः । सादया यज्ञं सुकृतस्य योनाविति (*sīda hotaḥ sva u loke cikitvānityagnirvai devānāṃ hotā tasyaiṣa svo loko yaduttaravedīnābhiḥ | sādayā yajñaṃ sukṛtasya yonāviti*)
यजमानो वै यज्ञो यजमानाय्नैवैतामाशिषमाशास्ते । देवावीर्देवान् हविषा यजास्यग्ने बृहद्यजमाने वयो धा इति प्राणो वै (*yajamāno vai yajño yajamānāynaivaitāmāśiṣamāśāste | devāvīrdevān haviṣā yajāsyagne bṛhadyajamāne vayo dhā iti prāṇo vai*)
वयः प्राणमेव तद्यजमाने दधाति ॥ (*vayaḥ prāṇameva tadyajamāne dadhāti ‖*)

Thereafter the hotṛ proceeds and, in this part of the rite, recites the sixth ṛk, which is to be said—

(6) सीद होतः स्व उ लोके चिकित्वान्तादया यज्ञं सुकृतस्य योन् । (*sīda hotaḥ sva u loke cikitvāntādayā yajñaṃ sukṛtasya yon |*)
देवावीर्देवान् हविषा यजास्यग्ने बृहद्यजमाने वयो धाः ॥ (*devāvīrdevān haviṣā yajāsyagne bṛhadyajamāne vayo dhāḥ ‖*)
(Ma. Saṃ. 3-29-8)

Agni is the priest called hotṛ of the gods; and the navel of the northern altar is Agni's own place. Therefore the prayer in the first pāda is: "O Agni, you are the accomplisher of the homa, the hotṛ of the gods; being such, sit in your own place, the northern altar." In the second pāda the word यज्ञ (*yajña*) means the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice. The meaning of the second pāda is: "O Agni, establish this sacrificer in the place of those who perform meritorious acts, the virtuous (sukṛta)." In the second half, the word वयः (*vayaḥ*) means life-breath (prāṇa). Therefore: "O Agni, dear to the gods, worship and satisfy the gods with the oblation, and bestow (that is, increase) life-breath, that is, length of life, in the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice." According to the Śruti sentence (यावदस्मिन् शरीरे प्राणो वसति तावदायुः (*yāvadasmin śarīre prāṇo vasati tāvadāyuḥ*), "as long as the life-breath dwells in this body, so long is there life"), as long as the life-breath

### Printed p. 548

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 548.*

remains in the body a man has life; since this is said, the word prāṇa is used here in the sense of "life". The purport is: "Grant long life to the sacrificer."

नि होता होतृषदने विदान इत्यग्निर्वै देवानां होता तस्यैतद्दोतृषदनं यदुत्तरवेदीनाभिः । त्वेषो दीदिना असदत्सुदक्ष इत्यासन्नू हि (*ni hotā hotṛṣadane vidāna ityagnirvai devānāṃ hotā tasyaitaddotṛṣadanaṃ yaduttaravedīnābhiḥ | tveṣo dīdinā asadatsudakṣa ityāsannū hi*)
स तर्हि भवति । अदब्धव्रतप्रमतिर्वसिष्ठ इत्यग्निर्वै देवानां वसिष्ठः । सहस्रं (*sa tarhi bhavati | adabdhavratapramatirvasiṣṭha ityagnirvai devānāṃ vasiṣṭhaḥ | sahasraṃ*)
भरः शुचिजिह्वो अग्निरित्येषा ह वा अस्य सहस्रंभरता यदेनमेकं संतं (*bharaḥ śucijihvo agnirityeṣā ha vā asya sahasraṃbharatā yadenamekaṃ saṃtaṃ*)
बहुधा विहरंति प्र ह वै साहस्रं पोषमाप्नोति य एवं वेद ॥ (*bahudhā viharaṃti pra ha vai sāhasraṃ poṣamāpnoti ya evaṃ veda ‖*)

The hotṛ recites, as the seventh ṛk—

(7) नि होता होतृषदने विदानस्त्वेषो दीदिना असदत्सुदक्षः । (*ni hotā hotṛṣadane vidānastveṣo dīdinā asadatsudakṣaḥ |*)
अदब्धव्रतप्रमतिर्वसिष्ठः सहस्रंभरः शुचिजिह्वो अग्निः ॥ (*adabdhavratapramatirvasiṣṭhaḥ sahasraṃbharaḥ śucijihvo agniḥ ‖*)
(Ma. Saṃ. 2-9-1)

this mantra. Agni is the hotṛ of the gods. The place of the navel of the northern altar is the place called hotṛṣadana, where this divine hotṛ Agni sits: this is the meaning of the first pāda. The second pāda means that this Agni, who is radiant and skilled in sacrificial acts, is seated in this northern-altar navel called hotṛṣadana. Since Agni carries the oblation to the gods, he has obtained the best place in heaven, the gods' own abode. This Agni, whose mind is bent on unharmed sacrifices and the like, is one who has an excellent dwelling: this is the meaning of the third pāda. The meaning of the word सहस्रंभर (*sahasraṃbhara*) is that, although Agni appears in a single form, he is present in all, the priests and the rest, and performs the duty of each, and so bears many forms; or, that Agni, being in many sacrificial houses and fulfilling the sacrifices of many sacrificers, bears many forms. Because his tongue (flame) is purified by receiving the oblation and the like, Agni is called शुचिजिह्व (*śucijihva*). It is praised here that such an Agni gives the sacrificer nourishment by the thousand, in cattle, gold and the like.

त्वं दूतस्त्वमु नः परस्पा इत्युत्तमया परिदधाति त्वं वस्य आ वृषभ प्रणेता । (*tvaṃ dūtastvamu naḥ paraspā ityuttamayā paridadhāti tvaṃ vasya ā vṛṣabha praṇetā |*)
अग्ने तोकस्य नस्तने तनूनामप्रयुच्छन्विद्यद्बोधिं गोपा इति अग्निर्वै (*agne tokasya nastane tanūnāmaprayucchanvidyadbodhiṃ gopā iti agnirvai*)
देवानां गोपा अग्निमेव तत्सर्वतो गोप्तारं परिदत्त आत्मने च (*devānāṃ gopā agnimeva tatsarvato goptāraṃ paridatta ātmane ca*)
यजमानाय च यत्रैवं विद्वानेतया परिदधात्यथो संवत्सरीणामे- (*yajamānāya ca yatraivaṃ vidvānetayā paridadhātyatho saṃvatsarīṇāme-*)
नैतां स्वस्तिं कुरुते ॥ (*naitāṃ svastiṃ kurute ‖*)

Next the hotṛ recites the last, the eighth ṛk, and brings the Agni-praṇayana rite to its close—

### Printed p. 549

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 549.*

(8) त्वं दूतस्त्वमु नः परस्पास्त्वं वस्य आ वृषभ प्रणेता । (*tvaṃ dūtastvamu naḥ paraspāstvaṃ vasya ā vṛṣabha praṇetā |*)
अग्ने तोकस्य नस्तने तनूनामप्रयुच्छन् दीद्यद्वोधि गोपाः ॥ (*agne tokasya nastane tanūnāmaprayucchan dīdyadvodhi gopāḥ ‖*)
(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-9 [the reference is cut off in the supplied text]) (Ma. Saṃ. 9-2)

This is the last ṛk in the Agni-praṇayana rite. Generally the last ṛk of a rite is called the paridhāna (covering) of that rite. According to the Śruti sentence अग्निर्देवानां दूत आसीत् (*agnirdevānāṃ dūta āsīt*) ("Agni was the messenger of the gods"), the meaning of the first pāda is: "O Agni, you are the messenger of the gods; you are our protector." Having recited this, the hotṛ recites the mantra that marks the completion of the Agni-praṇayana rite. "O Agni, you are the best among gods or among men; you have your dwelling in all places; you are the impeller in many rites; watching over our sons, grandsons and the rest, and over ourselves, so that no mishap may befall, you protect us." This is the main sense of the ṛk. Agni is the protector even of the gods; he brings protection to us and to the sacrificer. The man who knows this obtains protection from Agni for a whole year and lives in well-being.

ता एता अष्टावन्वाह रूपसमृद्धा एतद्वै यज्ञस्य समृद्धं यद्रूपसमृद्धं (*tā etā aṣṭāvanvāha rūpasamṛddhā etadvai yajñasya samṛddhaṃ yadrūpasamṛddhaṃ*)
यॆत्कर्म क्रियामाणमृगभिवदति । तासां त्रिः प्रथमामन्वाह त्रिरुत्तमां (*yètkarma kriyāmāṇamṛgabhivadati | tāsāṃ triḥ prathamāmanvāha triruttamāṃ*)
ता द्वादश संपद्यंते द्वादश वै मासाः संवत्सरः संवत्सरः प्रजापतिः (*tā dvādaśa saṃpadyaṃte dvādaśa vai māsāḥ saṃvatsaraḥ saṃvatsaraḥ prajāpatiḥ*)
प्रजाप्रत्यायतनाभिरेवाभी राध्योति य एवं वेद त्रिः प्रथमां त्रिरुत्त- (*prajāpratyāyatanābhirevābhī rādhyoti ya evaṃ veda triḥ prathamāṃ trirutta-*)
मामन्वाह यज्ञस्यैव तद्बर्सो नह्यति स्थेन्ने बलायाविस्रंसाय ॥ (*māmanvāha yajñasyaiva tadbarso nahyati sthenne balāyāvisraṃsāya ‖*)
(Ai. Brā. 5-2)

In this way the hotṛ recites the eight ṛks stated above. All of these together form a rite that has obtained a complete form. By saying at the proper times mantras that describe and explain the meaning of the respective acts, the sacrificial act is helped to be completed in full. Of these eight mantras the hotṛ recites the first and the last ṛks three times each. Thereby there come to be twelve ṛks in all in this rite. Twelve months make one year. Prajāpati is of the nature of the year. Therefore whoever knows the nature and the order of these mantras, which are established in Prajāpati, will prosper. Because the hotṛ recites the first and the last ṛks three times each, it is as though the two ends of the sacrifice were tied firmly so that they do not slip and fall.

The Agni-praṇayana rite is concluded.

### Printed p. 550

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 550.*

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्वं दू॒तस्त्वमु॑ नः पर॒स्पास्त्वं वस्य॒ आ वृ॑षभ प्रणे॒ता ।
> अग्ने॑ तो॒कस्य॑ न॒स्तने॑ त॒नूना॒मप्र॑युच्छ॒न्दीद्य॑द्बोधि गो॒पाः
> ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> त्वं । दूतः । त्वं । ऊं इति । नः । परःपाः । त्वं । वस्यः । आ । वृषभ । प्रनेता ।
> अग्ने । तोकस्य । नः । तने । तनूनां । अप्रयुच्छन् । दीद्यत् । जोधि । गोपाः
> ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वृषभ अभिमतफलवर्षक हे अग्ने त्वं दूतोऽस्मदीये यज्ञे
> देवाह्वानार्थं दूतो भव । त्वमु त्वमेव नोऽस्माकं परस्पा अपद्भ्यः
> पारयिता पाता च भव । किंच त्वं वस्यो वसुनो धनस्य आ प्रणीतास्मदाभि-
> मुख्येन प्रापयिता भव । अप्रयुच्छन् अप्रमाद्यन् । दीद्यत् ॥ दीदय-
> तिर्दिप्तिकर्मा ॥ शोभमानः । त्वं नोऽस्माकं तोकस्य पुत्रस्य तने ।
> तनोति विस्तारयति कुलमिति तनः पौत्रः । तस्मिन् अस्मदीयानां तनूनां गोपा
> गोपायिता । तथा च ब्राह्मणं । अग्निर्वै देवानां गोपा अग्निमेव तत्स-
> र्वतो गोप्तारं परिदत्त आत्मने च यजमानाय च । ऐ.ब्रा. १. २८. । इति ।
> तादृशस्त्वं च गोपायिता भवामीति बोधि । बुध्यस्व ॥

*Gist:* O showerer Agni, be our messenger to the gods, our rescuer from dangers, and the one who brings us wealth; shining, be the guardian of our sons, grandsons and bodies, as the Brāhmaṇa also says.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ವೃಷಭ (*vṛṣabha*)—O one who showers the desired objects. ಅಗ್ನೇ (*agne*)—O Agni. ತ್ವಂ (*tvaṃ*)—you. ದೂತಃ (*dūtaḥ*)—be the messenger (to our sacrifice) who calls the gods. ತ್ವಮು (*tvamu*)—you alone. ನಃ (*naḥ*)—to us. ಪರಸ್ಪಾಃ (*paraspāḥ*)—be the protector from calamity. ತ್ವಂ (*tvaṃ*)—you. ವಸ್ಯಃ (*vasyaḥ*)—of wealth (the wealth). ಆ ಪ್ರಣೇತಾ (*ā praṇetā*)—be the one who brings it together towards us. ಅಪ್ರಯುಚ್ಛನ್ (*aprayucchan*)—having a steady gaze. ದೀದ್ಯತ್ (*dīdyat*)—and shining. ನಃ (*naḥ*)—our. ತೋಕಸ್ಯ (*tokasya*)—of sons. ತನೇ (*tane*)—of grandsons. ತನೂನಾಂ (*tanūnāṃ*)—of our own bodies. ಗೋಪಾಃ (*gopāḥ*)—be the protector.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, giver of the desired objects: be the messenger who calls the gods to our sacrifice, the protector against calamities, the one who provides us with wealth, one whose gaze is free from heedlessness [the supplied text reads ಪ್ರಮಾವರಹಿತವಾದ, apparently for ಪ್ರಮಾದರಹಿತವಾದ; left as supplied], shining, and protector of our sons and grandsons and likewise of our own bodies.

### Printed p. 551

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 551.*

**Printed English translation**

Showerer(of benefits), Agni, be our messenger(to the gods); our preserver from evil; the conveyer to us of wealth; the protector of our sons and grandsons, and of our persons; understand(our prayer), ever heedful and resplendent.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ದೂತಃ (*dūtaḥ*)—In accordance with the Śruti sentence अग्निर्देवानां दूत आसीत् (*agnirdevānāṃ dūta āsīt*) ("Agni was the messenger of the gods"), Agni is said to be the messenger between gods and men, because he performs such tasks as invoking the gods in the sacrificial rites of the human sacrificers, conveying to them the oblations offered in the fire, and presenting to the gods the hymns and prayers of the ṛtvij priests.

ಪರಸ್ಪಾಃ (*paraspāḥ*)—अस्माकं आपद्भ्यः पारयिता पाता च (*asmākaṃ āpadbhyaḥ pārayitā pātā ca*): one who rescues and protects us from calamities of many kinds and from fear of enemies.

ವೃಷಭ (*vṛṣabha*)—अभिमतफलवर्षक (*abhimataphalavarṣaka*): one who showers the desired objects on us like rain, that is, one who gives; this is the meaning.

ತೋಕಸ್ಯ, ತನೇ (*tokasya, tane*)—The word तोक (*toka*) is read among the fifteen words for offspring (Ni. 3-2); therefore तोक (*toka*) means a son. The word तनय (*tanaya*) is read in the same place and also means a son; but when तनय (*tanaya*) or तन (*tana*) occurs, especially when both words occur in one ṛk, it is customary to explain it as grandson. Yāska, on this point, quotes the ṛk

ಯಾ ತೇ ದಿದ್ಯುದವಸೃಷ್ಟಾ ದಿವಸ್ಪರಿ ಕ್ಷ್ಮಯಾ ಚರತಿ ಪರಿ ಸಾ ವೃಣಕ್ತು ನಃ । ಸಹಸ್ರಂ ತೇ ಸ್ವಪಿನಾತ ಭೇಷಜಾ ಮಾನಸ್ತೋಕೇಷು ತನಯೇಷು ರೀರಿಷಃ ॥ (Ma. Saṃ. 7-46-3)

and explains the words तोकेषु (*tokeṣu*) and तनयेषु (*tanayeṣu*) occurring in it as पुत्रेषु च पौत्रेषु च (*putreṣu ca pautreṣu ca*) (Ni. 10-7). Sāyaṇa explains the word तने (*tane*) as तनोति विस्तारयति कुलमिति तनः पौत्रः (*tanoti vistārayati kulamiti tanaḥ pautraḥ*): that is, this word, formed from the root तनु विस्तारे (*tanu vistāre*), means one who extends the family by increasing it, hence तन (*tana*), a grandson (pautra).

### Printed p. 552

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 552.*

Therefore, according to Yāska's view, the word तोक (*toka*) can be taken to mean a son, and तन (*tana*) or तनय (*tanaya*) to mean a pautra, that is, a grandson.

ನಃ ತನೂನಾಂ ಗೋಪಾಃ (*naḥ tanūnāṃ gopāḥ*)—अस्मदीयानां तनूनां गोपा गोपायिता (*asmadīyānāṃ tanūnāṃ gopā gopāyitā*): explaining this as "one who protects our bodies, that is, us", Sāyaṇa quotes the Brāhmaṇa sentence अग्निर्वै देवानां गोपा अग्निमेव तत्सर्वतो गोप्तारं परिदत्त आत्मने च यजमानाय च (*agnirvai devānāṃ gopā agnimeva tatsarvato goptāraṃ paridatta ātmane ca yajamānāya ca*) (Ai. Brā. 1-28). The meaning of this Brāhmaṇa sentence we have already explained earlier, in the Agni-praṇayana rite (page 449).

Kaṇṭhanātha [the supplied text has only the name, with no content following it].

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वि॒धेम॑ ते पर॒मे जन्म॑न्नग्ने वि॒धेम॒ स्तोमै॒रव॑रे स॒धस्थे॑ ।
> यस्मा॒द्योने॑रु॒दारि॑था॒ यजे॒ तं प्र त्वे ह॒वींषि॑ जुहुरे॒ समि॑द्धे ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> विधेम । ते । परमे । जन्मन् । अग्ने । विधेम। स्तोमैः । अवरे । सधस्थे ।
> यस्मात् । योनेः । उत्आरिथ । यजे । तं । प्र । त्वे इति । हवींषि । जहुरे ।
> संइद्दे ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हॆ आग्ने परम उत्कृष्टे जन्मन्! जायते प्रादुर्भवत्यस्मिन्निति जन्मस्थानं
> द्यौः । तस्मिन् द्युलोकेऽवस्थिताय ते तुभ्यं विधेम । स्तुतिभिः परिचरेम ।
> अवरे द्युलोकादधस्तने सदस्येऽंतरिक्षे स्थिताय तुभ्यं स्तोमैः स्तोत्रैर्विधेम ।
> परिचरेम । यस्माद्योनेः पृथिवीरूपात्स्थानात् त्वमुदारिथ उद्धतोऽसि प्रादु-
> र्भूतोऽसि तं पृथिवीप्रदेशं यजे । पूजयामि । तत्र हि समिद्धे समिद्धिः
> समिध्यमाने त्वे त्वयि हवींषि पुरोडाशादीनि प्रजुहुरे । अध्वर्याद्वयः
> प्रजुह्वति । जुहोतेर्लिटि रूपं ॥

*Gist:* Agni is worshipped with hymns in his highest birthplace, heaven, and in the lower seat, the atmosphere; and on earth, his place of origin, the oblations are offered into him when kindled.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ಅಗ್ನೇ (*agne*)—O Agni. ಪರಮೇ (*parame*)—in the most excellent heaven. ಜನ್ಮನ್ (*janman*)—having your origin. ತೇ (*te*)—to you (you). ವಿಧೇಮ (*vidhema*)—we will serve with hymns. ಅವರೇ (*avare*)—in the lower one, below that. [The Pratipadārtha continues on the next page of the print.]

### Printed p. 553

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 553.*

[The Pratipadārtha of Ṛk 3 continues from the previous page.]

ಸಧಸ್ಥೇ (*sadhasthe*)—you who are in the atmosphere. ಸ್ತೋಮೈಃ (*stomaiḥ*)—with hymns. ವಿಧೇಮ (*vidhema*)—we will serve. ಯಸ್ಮಾದ್ಯೋನೇಃ (*yasmādyoneḥ*)—the very earth which is your place of origin. ಉದಾರಿಥ (*udārittha*)—from there you have become manifest. ತಂ (*taṃ*)—you who are in the place of the earth. ಯಜೇ (*yaje*)—I worship. ಸಮಿದ್ಧೇ (*samiddhe*)—when you are kindled. ತ್ವೇ (*tve*)—in you. ಹವೀಂಷಿ (*havīṃṣi*)—oblations. ಪ್ರ ಜುಹುರೇ (*pra juhure*)—they offer.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, I worship with hymns you who were born in the most excellent world of heaven, who are in the atmosphere, and who have the earth as the womb, your place of origin. And when you are kindled, the priests such as the adhvaryu offer oblations to you.

**Printed English translation**

We adore you, Agni, in your loftiest birth-(place), and with hymns in your inferior station; I worship that seat whence you have issued; the priests have offered you, when kindled, oblations.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this Ṛk Agni is described as born in heaven, the atmosphere and the earth. The same matter is described, with a slight difference, in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-95-3 also (as printed: "ಮ. ಸಂ."):

त्रिणी जाना परि भूषंत्यस्य समुद्र एकं दिव्येकमप्सु । पूर्वामनु प्र दिशं प्रार्थिवानामृतून्प्रशासद्वि दधावनुष्टु ॥ (*triṇī jānā pari bhūṣaṃtyasya samudra ekaṃ divyekamapsu | pūrvāmanu pra diśaṃ prārthivānāmṛtūnpraśāsadvi dadhāvanuṣṭu ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-95-3)

Here the births of Agni are stated one by one: त्रीणि जाना त्रिसंख्याकानि (*trīṇi jānā trisaṃkhyākāni*), that is, three kinds of births. This description shows the original nature of Agni's manifestation. That Agni's threefold existence, and the corresponding threefold manifestation, reflect the fundamental principle of the world is known from the explanation of the Bṛhaddevatā:

⟦ಭವದ್ಭೂತಸ್ಯ ಭವ್ಯಸ್ಯ ಜಂಗಮಸ್ಥಾವರಸ್ಯ ಚ ।
ಅಸ್ಯೈಕೇ ಸೂರ್ಯಮೇವೈಕಂ ಪ್ರಭವಂ ಪ್ರಲಯಂ ವಿದುಃ ॥
ಏತದ್ಭೂತೇಷು ಲೋಕೇಷು ಅಗ್ನಿಭೂತಂ ಸ್ಥಿತಂ ತ್ರಿಧಾ ।
ಋಷಯೋ ಗೀರ್ಭಿರರ್ಚಂತಿ ವ್ಯಂಜಿತಂ ನಾಮಭಿಸ್ತ್ರಿಭಿಃ ॥⟧

(Bṛ. De. 1-61; 1-64)

In these verses, in describing the greatness of Agni, the authors of the Bṛhaddevatā say that Prajāpati, the womb of all beings, has taken his existence in three ways, in the form of Agni, in three places, as the source of the creation and dissolution of all moving and unmoving beings

### Printed p. 554

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 554.*

of the past, present and future [text continued from the previous page]. The sages also speak of this threefold manifestation of Agni by three names, as seen below; continuing the praise of the same Agni:

⟦ತ್ರಿಸ್ಥಾನಂ ಚೈನಮರ್ಚಂತಿ ಹೋತ್ರಾಯಾಂ ವ್ಯಕ್ತಬರ್ಹಿಷಃ ॥
ಇಹೈವ ಪವಮಾನೋಽಗ್ನಿರ್ಮಧ್ಯಮೋಽಗ್ನಿರ್ವನಸ್ಪತಿಃ ।
ಅಮುಷ್ಮಿನ್ನೇವ ವಿಪ್ರೈಸ್ತು ಲೋಕೇಽಗ್ನಿಃ ಶುಚಿರುಚ್ಯತೇ ॥
ಇಹಾಗ್ನಿಭೂತಸ್ತ್ವೃಷಿಭಿರ್ಲೋಕೇ ಸ್ತುತಿಭಿರೀಳಿತಃ ।
ಜಾತವೇದಾಃ ಸ್ತುತೋ ಮಧ್ಯೆ ಸ್ತುತೋ ವೈಶ್ವಾನರೋ ದಿವಿ ॥⟧

(Bṛ. De. 1-65 to 67)

Among these three kinds, the Agni who manifests on the earth is known as Pavamāna-agni, the Agni of the middle world as Vanaspati, and the Agni who is in heaven as Śuci. These three divisions are read in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (2-2-4) under the division Pavamāna, Śuci, Pāvaka. Besides, the names Agni, Jātavedas and Vaiśvānara also indicate the three places and the three kinds of forms. The Nirukta-writer also, citing the Ṛk of the Ṛk-saṃhitā:

⟦ಸ್ತೋಮೇನ ಹಿ ದಿವಿ ದೇವಾಸೋ ಅಗ್ನಿಮಜೀಜನಇೃಕ್ತಿಭೀ ರೋದಸಿಪ್ರಾಂ ।
ತಮೂ ಅಕೃಣ್ವನ್ ತ್ರೇಧಾ ಭುವೇ ಕಂ ಸ ಓಷಧೀಃ ಪಚತಿ ವಿಶ್ವರೂಪಃ ॥⟧

(Ma. Saṃ. 10-88-10, as printed)

says: तमकुर्वंस्त्रेधाभावाय पृथिव्यामंतरिक्षे दिवीति शाकपूणिः (*tamakurvaṃstredhābhāvāya pṛthivyāmaṃtarikṣe divīti śākapūṇiḥ*). Following the explanation of Śākapūṇi, he explains clearly, by the examples of Saṃhitā sentences, that in these three places, with the divisions earth, atmosphere and heaven, Agni has manifested in three forms, Agni, Jātavedas and Vaiśvānara (Ni. 7-14 to 7-25).

In the same way in this Ṛk also the three places are described in order by the names समुद्रे (*samudre*), दिवि (*divi*), अप्पु (*appu*) [sic, as supplied]: in the ocean as the submarine fire, in heaven as the Āditya (sun), and in अप्पु (*appu*), that is, the atmosphere, as lightning. Although the names of the places differ now and then, the meaning is all one. For example, here the ocean is put in place of the earth, divided according to this context. In sum, these three divisions have been spoken of in the four Vedas in various contexts.

### Printed p. 555

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 555.*

⟦ದಿವಸ್ಪರಿ ಪ್ರಥಮಂ ಜಜ್ಞೇ ಅಗ್ನಿರಸ್ಮದ್ ದ್ವಿತೀಯಂ ಪರಿ ಜಾತವೇದಾಃ ।
ತೃತೀಯಮಪ್ಸು ನೃಮಣಾ ಅಜಸ್ರಮಿಂಧಾನ ಏನಂ ಜರತೇ ಸ್ವಾಧೀಃ ॥⟧

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-45-1; Vā. Saṃ. 12-18; Tai. Sa. 1-3-14-5)

⟦ಅಗ್ನಿರಸ್ಮಿ ಜನ್ಮನಾ ಜಾತವೇದಾ ಧೃತಂ ಮೇ ಚಕ್ಷುರಮೃತಂ ಮ ಆಸನ್ ।
ಅರ್ಕಸ್ತ್ರಿಧಾತೂ ರಜಸೋ ವಿಮಾನೋಽಜಸ್ರೋ ಘರ್ಮೋ ಹವಿರಸ್ಮಿ ನಾಮ ॥⟧

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-26-7; Vā. Saṃ. 18-16; Ni. 14-2)

From such explanations the nature of the description त्रीणि जाना (*trīṇi jānā*) is clearly known.

इदमुक्तं भवति । न साधारणवत्सवदयमग्निर्वत्सो दृश्यमानो मातुरुदरे वर्धते तर्हि दृश्यमान एव वर्धते । पशुवत्तिरश्चीनानां अब्रूपमात्यणामुदरे च तिरश्चिनो न वर्धते तर्हि ऊर्ध्वः सन् वर्धते । ता इव चायं न दीप्तिहीनस्तर्हि स्वयशाः स्वायत्तदीप्तिर्भवति । त्वष्टुस्तु यदायमग्निर्जातस्तदा व्यावापृथिव्यावपि तस्माद्भीत्वा परागते । पश्चात्पुनरागत्य तं सिंहसदृशमग्निं बालवत्सेवेते स्मेति भावः ॥ (*idamuktaṃ bhavati | na sādhāraṇavatsavadayamagnirvatso dṛśyamāno māturudare vardhate tarhi dṛśyamāna eva vardhate | paśuvattiraścīnānāṃ abrūpamātyaṇāmudare ca tiraścino na vardhate tarhi ūrdhvaḥ san vardhate | tā iva cāyaṃ na dīptihīnastarhi svayaśāḥ svāyattadīptirbhavati | tvaṣṭustu yadāyamagnirjātastadā vyāvāpṛthivyāvapi tasmādbhītvā parāgate | paścātpunarāgatya taṃ siṃhasadṛśamagniṃ bālavatsevete smeti bhāvaḥ ‖*) [Sanskrit as supplied; the words अब्रूपमात्यणामुदरे (*abrūpamātyaṇāmudare*) look garbled in the supplied text.] This means: unlike ordinary infants, this Agni did not grow hidden inside, unseen by anyone, in the mother's womb; he grows visibly to all (by the burning of wood and the like). Further, he did not grow hidden in the womb of the water-formed mothers, as animals do; he grows visibly, with upward-turned flames, that is, flames rising upwards. Nor is he without brightness like the water, his mothers; he shines with self-luminosity. Seeing Agni thus born with self-luminosity, heaven and earth were frightened, but they came again to him, who is like a lion, and served him: this is the sense.

Here the nature of the lightning-Agni is described. Praising the power of lightning, which at one and the same time has a heart-attracting loveliness and terrifying fearsome qualities, the description is given. The fire of lightning, which appears shining and of beautiful splendour in the midst of the waters of rain in the form of waves, is exceedingly lovely as soon as it is born. At the very same time the power of that lightning, surpassing all forces, is as terrifying as a lion when it overruns the world with unchecked speed and burning. For that very reason heaven and earth both pay homage to this Agni and serve him. Then it may be that not only the lightning-fire but also the earthly fire is described with these qualities [the sentence is obscure in the supplied text]. These differences of meaning can be understood from the explanation.

### Printed p. 556

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 556.*

[The Special Topics of Ṛk 3 continue; the Sanskrit of the commentator's explanation, begun on the previous page, ends here.] अत्रेदमुक्तं भवति । यदा मेघोदकरूपाभिर्गोभिर्युक्तः सन्नग्निर्वैद्युतः स्वकीयमुत्पृष्टतरं तेजस्तेजःपुंजपूर्णेंतरिक्षे प्रादुर्भावयति तदा तत्सर्वमंतरिक्षं तेन तेजसा प्रमृष्टं भवति यच्च तत्स्वरूपप्रादुर्भवनं तदेव देवांतरैः सहाग्नेर्गमनं भवतीति । (*atredamuktaṃ bhavati | yadā meghodakarūpābhirgobhiryuktaḥ sannagnirvaidyutaḥ svakīyamutpṛṣṭataraṃ tejastejaḥpuṃjapūrṇeṃtarikṣe prādurbhāvayati tadā tatsarvamaṃtarikṣaṃ tena tejasā pramṛṣṭaṃ bhavati yacca tatsvarūpaprādurbhavanaṃ tadeva devāṃtaraiḥ sahāgnergamanaṃ bhavatīti |*) Its sense, as the Kannada explains: when Agni, in the form of lightning in the midst of the clouds, appears in the atmosphere with his full brightness, shining, then through the light of Agni the whole atmosphere is filled with brightness, that is, it shines exceedingly. In this way Agni, having manifested, joins the other gods: this is the sense.

Both the first half and the second half of this Ṛk admit of two kinds of construction. For the first half: first, that Agni, joining with the rain-waters in the atmosphere, causes his brightness to arise in the form of lightning; and second, that in the sacrificial house, joined with praises and with soma juices, he produces his blazing splendour. Such are the senses that may be taken.

In this way the idea that Agni has births in heaven, the atmosphere and the earth is explained in this Ṛk.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अग्ने॒ यज॑स्व ह॒विषा॒ यजी॑यान्भृ॒ष्टी[?] दे॒ष्णम॒भि गृ॑णीहि॒ राधः॑ ।
> त्वं ह्यसि॑ रयि॒पती॑ रयी॒णां त्वं शु॒क्रस्य॒ वच॑सो म॒नोता॑ ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अग्ने । यजस्व । हविषा । यजीयान् । श्रुष्टी[?] । देष्णं । अभि । गृणीहि । राधः ।
> त्वं । हि । असि । रयिऽपतिः । रयीणां । त्वं । शुक्रस्य । वचसः । मनोता ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने यजीयान् यष्टृतमस्त्वं हविषास्मद्दत्तेन यजस्व । देवान्
> संयोजय । देष्णं देयं राधोऽन्नं श्रुष्टी ॥श्रुष्टीति क्षिप्रनाम॥क्षिप्रमभिगृणीहि ।

*Gist:* Agni, the best sacrificer, is to sacrifice with our oblation and quickly announce to the gods the food (or to us the wealth) to be given.

### Printed p. 557

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1, Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 557.*

> देवानां पुरत इदं स्वादुतरमिदं स्वादुतरमिति ब्रूहि । यद्वा श्रुष्टी क्षिप्रकारी त्वं
> देष्णं देयं राधो धनमभिगृणीहि । अस्मदाभिमुख्येन ब्रूहि । प्रयच्छेत्यर्थः ।
> दानसामर्थ्यं दर्शयति । त्वं रयीणां धनानां मध्यॆ रयिपतिरुत्तमस्य धनस्य पतिरसि
> हि । यद्वा रयीणां रयिपतिरित्यत्रैको रयिशब्दोनुवादः । त्वं शुक्रस्य दीप्तस्य
> वचसोऽस्मदीयस्य स्तोत्रस्य मनोता प्रज्ञाता भवसि । यद्वा मानयिता ॥

*Gist (continued):* he is lord of the best wealth and the knower (or honourer) of our bright praise.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Agni (अग्ने (*agne*)): O Agni. Yajīyān (यजीयान् (*yajīyān*)): you, the best sacrificer. With the oblation (हविषा (*haviṣā*)): (given by us) with the oblation. Sacrifice (यजस्व (*yajasva*)): perform the sacrifice addressed to the gods. Deṣṇam (देष्णं (*deṣṇaṃ*)): that which must be offered (to the gods). Rādhaḥ (राधः (*rādhaḥ*)): the food. Śruṣṭī (श्रुष्टी (*śruṣṭī*)): quickly. Abhi gṛṇīhi (अभि गृणीहि (*abhi gṛṇīhi*)): proclaim (before them). [Or: Deṣṇam: that which must be given to us. Rādhaḥ: the wealth. Śruṣṭī: quickly. Abhi gṛṇīhi: make known to us.] You (त्वं (*tvaṃ*)): you. Rayāṇām [sic, as supplied; the Pada has रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*)] (रयाणां (*rayāṇāṃ*)): among riches. Rayipatiḥ (रयिपतिः (*rayipatiḥ*)): as the best lord of wealth. Asi hi (असि हि (*asi hi*)): are you not? You (त्वं (*tvaṃ*)): you. Śukrasya (शुक्रस्य (*śukrasya*)): shining. Vacasaḥ (वचसः (*vacasaḥ*)): of our hymn, the greatness. Manotā (मनोता (*manotā*)): you are the one who knows.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, you, the best sacrificer, sacrifice to the gods with the oblation given by us. Quickly proclaim before them the food that must be offered to the gods. And make known to us the wealth that must be given to us. You are the best lord of wealth, and the knower of the greatness of our shining praise.

**Printed English translation**

Agni, you are the chief of sacrificers; worship(the gods) with the oblation, and earnestly commend to them the(sacrificial) food that is to be given(them); for you, indeed, are the sovereign lord of riches; you are the appreciator of our pious prayer.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

राधः (*rādhaḥ*) (*rādhaḥ*): since the word राधः (*rādhaḥ*) is read among the twenty-eight names of wealth beginning with मघं (*maghaṃ*) रेक्णः (*rekṇaḥ*) (Ni. 3-9) [the supplied text reads परित (*parita*), "paritā", where "read" (paṭhita) would be expected; kept as supplied], राधः (*rādhaḥ*) means wealth. In this Ṛk Sāyaṇa gives the word two meanings, food and wealth.

श्रुष्टी (*śruṣṭī*): quickly. Yāska's explanation is that श्रुष्टीति क्षिप्रनाम (*śruṣṭīti kṣipranāma*) ("śruṣṭī is a name for 'quick'") (Ni. 6-13).

### Printed p. 558

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1 (printed "Adhyāya 1"), Sūkta 9, p. 558.*

रयीणां रयिपतिः (*rayīṇāṃ rayipatiḥ*): it means lord of wealth. Since the word रयिपतिः (*rayipatiḥ*) already means lord of wealth, the word रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*) is a mere repetition. Yet such repetitive usages are found here and there in the Ṛgveda, for example गणानां त्वा गणपतिं (*gaṇānāṃ tvā gaṇapatiṃ*), वसोरिंद्रं वसुपतिं (*vasoriṃdraṃ vasupatiṃ*), and so on.

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒भयं॑ ते॒ न क्षी॑यते वस॒व्यं॑ दि॒वेदि॑वे॒ जाय॑मानस्य दस्म ।
> कृ॒धि क्षु॒मंतं॑ जरि॒तार॑मग्ने कृ॒धि पतिं॑ स्वप॒त्यस्य॑ रा॒यः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उभयं । ते । न । क्षीयते । वसव्यं । दिनेदिवे । जायमानस्य । दस्म ।
> कृधि । क्षुमंतं । जरितारं । अग्ने । कृधि । पतिं । सुअपत्यस्य । रायः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> दस्म दर्शनीय हे अग्ने दिवेदिवे प्रतिदिनमग्निहोत्रकाले जायमानस्य ते
> तवोभयं वसव्यं दिव्यं भौमं च वसु ॥ वस्वादित्वात्स्वार्थिको यत्पत्ययः । पा-
> ५. ४. ३०. ४ ॥ तादृशमुभयं वसु न क्षीयते । न हीयते । अस्योभयविधधन-
> सद्भावे मंत्रवर्णः त्वं ह्यग्ने दिव्यस्य राजसि त्वं पार्थिवस्यॆत्यत्रोक्तः । अतस्त्वं
> जरितारं तव स्तोत्रं कुर्वाणमिमं यजमानं क्षुमंतमन्नवंतं कीर्तिमंतं ना
> कृधि । कुरु । किंच स्वपत्यस्य शोभनापत्ययुक्तस्य रायो धनस्य पतिं स्वामिनं कुरु ॥

*Gist:* Both treasures of Agni, divine and earthly, never fail; so make the praiser rich in food and renown, and lord of wealth with fine offspring.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Dasma (दस्म (*dasma*)): O one of beautiful form. Agni (अग्ने (*agne*)): O Agni. Dive-dive (दिवेदिवे (*divedive*)): every day. Jāyamānasya (जायमानस्य (*jāyamānasya*)): of you who are born. Te (ते (*te*)): your. Ubhayaṃ vasavyam (उभयं वसव्यं (*ubhayaṃ vasavyaṃ*)): the two dwelling-places in the form of heaven and earth, both. Na kṣiyate (न क्षियते (*na kṣiyate*)) [the Saṃhitā and Pada have क्षीयते (*kṣīyate*)]: are without destruction. Jaritāram (जरितारं (*jaritāraṃ*)): the sacrificer who praises you. Kṣumantam (क्षुमंतं (*kṣumaṃtaṃ*)): as one possessing food. Kṛdhi (कृधि (*kṛdhi*)): make. Svapatyasya (स्वपत्यस्य (*svapatyasya*)): joined with excellent offspring. Rāyaḥ (रायः (*rāyaḥ*)): of wealth. Patiṃ kṛdhi (पतिं कृधि (*patiṃ kṛdhi*)): make also the lord.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O beautiful-formed Agni, your two dwelling-places in the form of heaven and earth, you who are born every day, are both without destruction. Make the sacrificer who praises you a possessor of food, and make him also lord of wealth joined with excellent offspring.

[The printed English translation of this Ṛk is not present in the supplied text of these pages; it is not supplied here.]

### Printed p. 559

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 1; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 559.*

**Printed English translation (Ṛk 5)**

Beautiful Agni, the two dwelling-places(heaven and earth) of you, who are born day by day, never perish; confer upon him who praises you(abundant) food, and make him the master of riches,(supporting) virtuous offspring.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

दिवेदिवे जायमानस्य (दिवेदिवे जायमानस्य (*divedive jāyamānasya*)): "of him who is born every day, at the time of the Agnihotra." Because people kindle and worship Agni every day, at the Agnihotra time and at other times, he is said to be born every day. In the same way there are many descriptions of the goddess Uṣas and of the Sun being born every day.

जरितारं (जरितारं (*jaritāraṃ*)): the sacrificer who is praising you. The word जरितृ (जरितृ (*jaritṛ*)) is read among the names of praisers (Ni. 3-19).

उभयं वसव्यं (उभयं वसव्यं (*ubhayaṃ vasavyaṃ*)): the dwelling-place in heaven and earth. Two kinds of dwelling-place are meant here: in heaven, among the gods; on earth, in the sacrificial house of the sacrificers who perform sacrifices.

क्षुमंतं (क्षुमंतं (*kṣumaṃtaṃ*)): "as one possessing food". The word क्षु (क्षु (*kṣu*)) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). क्षुमंतं (क्षुमंतं (*kṣumaṃtaṃ*)) means one who has food.

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सैनानी॑केन सुवि॒दत्रो॑ अ॒स्मे यष्टा॑ दे॒वा आय॑जिष्ठः स्व॒स्ति ।
> अद॑ब्बो गो॒पा उ॒त नः॑ पर॒स्पा अग्ने॑ द्यु॒मदु॒त रे॒वद्दि॑ दीहि ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सः । एना । अनीकेन । सुविदत्रः । अस्मे इति । यष्टा । देवान् । आयजिष्ठः । स्वस्ति ।
> अदब्धः । गोपाः । उत । नः । परःपाः । आग्ने । द्युमत् । उत । रेवत् । दिदीहि ॥ ६ ॥

> *saḥ | enā | anīkena | suvidatraḥ | asme iti | yaṣṭā | devān | āyajiṣṭhaḥ | svasti |*
> *adabdhaḥ | gopāḥ | uta | naḥ | paraḥpāḥ | āgne | dyumat | uta | revat | didīhi ‖ 6 ‖*

### Printed p. 560

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 9, p. 560.*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> साकमेधे पर्वणि पूर्वद्युरनुसवनं तिस्र इष्टयः कर्तव्याः । प्रथमाया-
> मग्नेरनीकवतः सैनानीकेनेति याज्या । तथा ततः साकमेधा इति खंडे सूत्रितं ।
> सैनानीकेन सुविदत्रो अस्मे । आ. २. १८. । इति ॥
> हे अग्ने स तादृशस्त्वमेनानेनानीकेन युक्तस्त्वं सुविदत्रः सुप्रतिज्ञः
> सुधनो वास्के अस्माकं भव । देवान्यष्टा देवानां हविष्पदानेन पूजक आय-
> जिष्ठो यष्टृतमोऽदब्धः केनाप्यतिरस्कृतो गोपा देवानां गोपायिता । उत
> अपि च नोऽस्माकं परस्पा दुरितेभ्यः पारयिता पाता च त्वं द्युमत् कांतियुक्तं
> उत अपि च रेवत् धनयुक्तर यथा भवति तथा स्वस्ति दिदीहि । क्षेमेण सर्वतो
> दिदीहि । क्षेमेण सर्वतो दीप्यस्व ॥

*Gist:* At the Sākamedha, a Yājyā verse in the first iṣṭi (Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra 2-18); Agni, with his host, is asked to be bountiful, unassailable protector and preserver, and to shine with wealth and well-being.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Agni (अग्ने (*agne*)): O Agni. That one (सः (*saḥ*)): you, of the qualities described above. With this host (एना अनीकेन (*enā anīkena*)): together with all your attendants. To us (अस्मे (*asme*)): to us. Suvidatraḥ (सुविदत्रः (*suvidatraḥ*)): be the giver of excellent hope [the Kannada reads ಆಶಂಸೆ]. The gods (देवान् (*devān*)): the worshipper of the gods. Yaṣṭā (यष्टा (*yaṣṭā*)): the one who worships. Āyajiṣṭhaḥ (आयजिष्ठः (*āyajiṣṭhaḥ*)): the best even among worshippers. Adabdhaḥ (अदब्धः (*adabdhaḥ*)): one not despised by anyone. Gopāḥ (गोपाः (*gopāḥ*)): the protector (of the gods). And (उत (*uta*)): and. To us (नः (*naḥ*)): to us. Paraspāḥ (परस्पाः (*paraspāḥ*)): you who are also the deliverer from sin. Dyumat (द्युमत् (*dyumat*)): that which is bright. And (उत (*uta*)): and. Revat (रेवत् (*revat*)): that which is rich. Svasti (स्वस्ति (*svasti*)): so that well-being is obtained. Didīhi (दिदीहि (*didīhi*)): shine.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, most excellent one, together with all your attendants be the giver of excellent hope to us. You are the worshipper of the gods, the best among worshippers, unrejected by anyone, the protector, and the one who delivers us from sin: shine so that bright and wealthy well-being is obtained.

**Printed English translation**

Do you, with this(your) host(of attendants), be of good promise to us; be, the especial worshipper of the gods, their nnassailable [sic] protector, as well as our preserver; light up(all) with shining and affluent well-being.

### Printed p. 561

*Running head: Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 2; Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 561.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In the sacrifice called Sākamedha, three iṣṭis (sacrifices) must be performed on the day before the Savana. In the first iṣṭi, in the Yājyā offering addressed to Agni, the deity (printed ಅಗ್ನಿ ರನೀಕನಾನ್, as supplied, "Agni anīkavān"), this Ṛk beginning सैनानीकेन (सैनानीकेन (*sainānīkena*)) is employed, as explained by the sūtra सैनानीकेन सुविदत्रो अस्मे (सैनानीकेन सुविदत्रो अस्मे (*sainānīkena suvidatro asme*)) in the section beginning ततः साकमेधाः (ततः साकमेधा (*tataḥ sākamedhā*)) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (Ā. 2-18).

In this Ṛk, the conclusion of the sūkta, Agni is prayed to make us free of sin and to bestow wealth and well-being.

सुविदत्रः (सुविदत्रः (*suvidatraḥ*)): Sāyaṇa's explanation is सुप्रतिज्ञः सुधनो वा (सुप्रतिज्ञा सुधनो वा (*supratijñā sudhano vā*)) [as supplied, with ಸುಪ್ರತಿಜ್ಞಾ where the bhāṣya has ಸುಪ್ರತಿಜ್ಞಃ]: one of good promise, or one with wealth. Yāska:

सुविदत्रः कल्याणविद्यः । आग्ने याहि सुविदत्रेभिरर्वाज् इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*suvidatraḥ kalyāṇavidyaḥ | āgne yāhi suvidatrebhirarvāj ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 6-14)

That is, he gives the meaning of the word सुविदत्र (सुविदत्र (*suvidatra*)) as "of auspicious knowledge" or "of good knowledge", and gives the example beginning आग्ने याहि (आग्ने याहि (*āgne yāhi*)) [as supplied]. And further:

पूषा त्वेतश्चावयतु प्र विद्वाननष्टपशुर्भुवनस्य गोपाः । (*pūṣā tvetaścāvayatu pra vidvānanaṣṭapaśurbhuvanasya gopāḥ |*)
स त्वैतेभ्यः परि ददत्पितृभ्योग्निर्देवेभ्यः सुविदत्रियेभ्यः ॥ (*sa tvaitebhyaḥ pari dadatpitṛbhyognirdevebhyaḥ suvidatriyebhyaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-17-3)

In giving the nirvacana for this Ṛk he says:

सुविदत्रं धनं भवति विंदतेवैकोपसर्गाद्द दातेर्वा स्यात् द्व्यपसर्गात् । (*suvidatraṃ dhanaṃ bhavati viṃdatevaikopasargādda dātervā syāt dvyapasargāt |*) (Ni. 7-9)

That is, सुविदत्र (सुविदत्र (*suvidatra*)) means wealth. He explains that this word can be formed either from the root विंदति (विंदति (*viṃdati*)), "to obtain", with the single prefix सु (सु (*su*)), or from the root ददाति (ददाति (*dadāti*)), "to give", with the two prefixes सु (सु (*su*)) and वि (वि (*vi*)).

## Sūkta 10 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Introduction to the sūkta (Sanskrit, transcribed)**

> जोहूत्र इति षडृचं दशमं सूक्तं गार्त्समदं त्रैष्टुभमाग्नेयं जो-
> हूत्र इत्यनुक्रांतत्वात् । सूक्तविनियोगः पूर्वेण सहोक्तः ॥

*Gist:* *johūtra* is the tenth sūkta, six ṛks, by Gṛtsamada, deity Agni, Triṣṭubh; its application is as stated for the preceding sūkta.

**Anuvāda (Kannada)**

This sūkta जोहूत्रः (जोहूत्रः (*johūtraḥ*)) is the tenth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has six ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Gṛtsamada; the deity is Agni; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇī it is given as beginning with जोहूत्र (जोहूत्र (*johūtra*)). The application of this sūkta has been stated earlier.

*(The supplied text ends the page with a stray footer "[36] Vol. 14".)*

### Printed p. 562

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 10, p. 562.*

## Sūkta 10 (Maṇḍala 2)

**Heading of the sūkta (as supplied)**

Om. Sūkta 10. Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 10. Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 2. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Gṛtsamada. Deity: Agni. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> जो॒हूत्रो॑ अ॒ग्निः प्र॑थ॒मः पि॒तेवे॒ळस्प॒दे मनु॑षा॒ यत्समि॑द्धः।
> श्रियं॒ वसा॑नो अ॒मृतो॒ विचे॑ता मर्मृ॒जेन्यः॑ श्रव॒स्य१॒ः॑ स वा॒जी ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> जोहूत्रः । अग्निः । प्रथमः । पिता इव । इळॆः । पदे । मनुषा । यत् । संइद्धः ।
> श्रियं । वसानः । अमृतः । विचेताः । मर्मृजेन्यः । श्रवस्यः । सः । वाजी ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> जोहूत्रः सर्वैर्यज्ञार्थं ह्वातव्यो होतव्यो वा ॥ हूयतेर्जुहो-
> तेर्वा रूपं ॥ तादृशः प्रथमः । अग्निर्वै देवानामवम इत्याम्नानात् । मुख्यो
> यो अग्नि । यत् यदेळॆ इळायाः पद उत्तरवेद्यात्मके स्थाने मनुषा मनुष्येण
> यजमानेन समिद्धः सोऽग्निः पितेव देवानां हविष्प्रापणेन पालको भवति । श्रियं
> दीप्तिं वसान आच्छादयन् अमृतो मरणधर्मरहितो विचेता विविधप्रज्ञः श्रवस्यः ।
> श्रव इत्यन्ननाम । तस्मै हितः । वाजी बलवान् । सोऽग्निर्मर्मृजेन्यः सर्वैः
> परिचरणीयो भवति ॥ यण्णु गंतात्केन्य प्रत्ययः ॥

> *johūtraḥ sarvairyajñārthaṃ hvātavyo hotavyo vā ‖ hūyaterjuho-*
> *tervā rūpaṃ ‖ tādṛśaḥ prathamaḥ | agnirvai devānāmavama ityāmnānāt | mukhyo*
> *yo agni | yat yadeḷè iḷāyāḥ pada uttaravedyātmake sthāne manuṣā manuṣyeṇa*
> *yajamānena samiddhaḥ so 'gniḥ piteva devānāṃ haviṣprāpaṇena pālako bhavati | śriyaṃ*
> *dīptiṃ vasāna ācchādayan amṛto maraṇadharmarahito vicetā vividhaprajñaḥ śravasyaḥ |*
> *śrava ityannanāma | tasmai hitaḥ | vājī balavān | so 'gnirmarmṛjenyaḥ sarvaiḥ*
> *paricaraṇīyo bhavati ‖ yaṇṇu gaṃtātkenya pratyayaḥ ‖*

*Gist:* Agni, invoked or offered to by all, is first among the gods; kindled by the sacrificer on the northern altar he becomes like a father to the gods, immortal, wise, food-giving and strong, to be served by all. The grammatical tail (a kenya-suffix formation for मर्मृजेन्यः (*marmṛjenyaḥ*)) is not explained here.

### Printed p. 563

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Maṇḍala 2, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 2, p. 563.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

जोहूत्रः (*johūtraḥ*)—the one called by all, or worshipped. प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*)—chief (among all the gods). अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni. यत् (*yat*)—when. इळः (*iḷaḥ*)—of the earth. पदे (*pade*)—in the place that is the northern altar. मनुषा (*manuṣā*)—by man. समिद्धः (*samiddhaḥ*)—when he is kindled (then). पितेव (*piteva*)—he becomes like a father, the protector (of the gods, by carrying the oblation to them). श्रियं (*śriyaṃ*)—splendour. वसानः (*vasānaḥ*)—the one who wears. अमृतः (*amṛtaḥ*)—free from the nature of death. विचेताः (*vicetāḥ*)—possessed of manifold understanding. श्रवस्यः (*śravasyaḥ*)—the giver of food. वाजी (*vājī*)—mighty. सः (*saḥ*)—that Agni. मर्मजेन्यः (*marmajenyaḥ*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā and Pada read मर्मृजेन्यः (*marmṛjenyaḥ*))—is the one to be worshipped by all.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Agni, who is called by all for help and is chief among all the gods, when he is kindled by man on the northern altar, the place of the earth, becomes like a father, the protector of the gods, because he carries the oblation to them. Agni, who wears splendour, is free from the nature of death, has manifold understanding, gives food and is mighty, is the one to be worshipped by all.

**Printed English translation**

The adorable Agni, the first(of the gods), and their nourisher when kindled by man upon the altar; he who is clothed in radiance, is immortal, discriminating, food-bestowing, powerful; he is to be worshipped.

**Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu**

जोहूत्रः (*johūtraḥ*)—This word may be formed either from the root ह्वयति (*hvayati*) meaning "to call" or from the root जुहोति (*juhoti*) meaning "to offer in the fire". For this word the bhāṣya says सर्वैर्यज्ञार्थं ह्वातव्यो होतव्यो वा (*sarvairyajñārthaṃ hvātavyo hotavyo vā*): that is, one who is to be called by all for the sacrifice, or one to whom offerings are to be made. The sense is that at the sacrifice all the sacrificers invite and call Agni, and make offerings in Agni, directed to Agni and the other gods.

प्रथम: (*prathama:*) (as supplied)—Of the word प्रथम (*prathama*) Yāska says प्रथमः इति मुख्यनाम प्रतमो भवति (*prathamaḥ iti mukhyanāma pratamo bhavati*): the word means "chief"; or the explanation given is प्रथमः-प्रकृष्टतमः (*prathamaḥ-prakṛṣṭatamaḥ*), the most excellent (Ni. 2-22). In the present Ṛk प्रथमः (*prathamaḥ*) means that Agni is the chief or most excellent among the gods.

### Printed p. 564

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Anuvāka 1, Sūkta 10, p. 564.*

To support this matter the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa passage अग्निर्वै देवानामवमो विष्णुः परमः (*agnirvai devānāmavamo viṣṇuḥ paramaḥ*) (Ai. Brā. 1-1) is cited; that is, in the list of the gods Agni is the first and Viṣṇu the last. Besides, there is the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa passage अग्निरग्रे प्रथमो देवतानां । संयातानामुत्तमो विष्णुरासीत् (*agniragre prathamo devatānāṃ | saṃyātānāmuttamo viṣṇurāsīt*) (Tai. Brā. 2-4-3-3): among all the gods the first place belongs to Agni and the last place to Viṣṇu.

इळस्पदे (*iḷaspade*)—यत् यदेळ इळायाः पद उत्तरवेद्यात्मके स्थाने (*yat yadeḷa iḷāyāḥ pada uttaravedyātmake sthāne*). The word इळा (*iḷā*) is read among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5), among the names of speech (Ni. 2-23; as supplied "vājñāmagaḷu"), among the names of food (Ni. 3-9 as printed "3.9") and among the names of cows (Ni. 3-9). Also the etymology इळा ईट्टेःस्तुतिकर्मणः (*iḷā īṭṭeḥstutikarmaṇaḥ*) (as supplied) is given (Ni. 11-48): the word इळा (*iḷā*) is formed from the root ईट्टे (*īṭṭe*) (as supplied) meaning "to praise". Ordinarily the word इळा (*iḷā*) in इळस्पदे (*iḷaspade*) denotes the earth. इळस्पदे (*iḷaspade*) means the northern altar, the navel-place of the earth. On such a northern altar Agni is मनुषा समिद्धः (*manuṣā samiddhaḥ*), kindled and blazing, lit by a human sacrificer. The same sense is made plain in the verse

इळस्पदे समिध्यसे स नो वसून्याभर (*iḷaspade samidhyase sa no vasūnyābhara*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-191-1; as printed "ಋ ಸಂ.")

(the Kannada text calls it "mukku", apparently a misprint for ऋक्कु (*ṛkku*)).

विचेताः (*vicetāḥ*) (as supplied विचेता: (*vicetā:*))—विविधप्रज्ञः (*vividhaprajñaḥ*): one with manifold understanding, that is, the all-knowing.

मर्मृजेन्यः (*marmṛjenyaḥ*)—सर्वैः परिचरणीयः (*sarvaiḥ paricaraṇīyaḥ*): to be served by all, that is, by the sacrificer, the officiating priests and all others.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> शू॒या अ॒ग्निश्चि॒त्रभा॑नु॒र्हवं॑ मे॒ विश्वा॑भिर्गी॒र्भिर॒मृतो॒ विचे॑ताः ।
> श्या॒वा रथं॑ वहतो॒ रोहि॑ता वो॒तारु॒षाह॑ चक्रे॒ विभृ॑त्रः ॥ २ ॥

> *śūyā agniścitrabhānurhavaṃ me viśvābhirgīrbhiramṛto vicetāḥ |*
> *śyāvā rathaṃ vahato rohitā votāruṣāha cakre vibhṛtraḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

### Printed p. 565

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 2, p. 565.*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> श्रुयाः । अग्निः । चित्रभानुः । हवं । मे । विश्वाभिः । गीभिः । अमृतः । विचेताः ।
> श्यावा । रथं । वहतः । रोहिता । वा । उत । अरुषा । अह । चक्रे । विभृत्रः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अमृतो मरणधर्मरहितो विचेता विशिष्टप्रज्ञश्चित्रभानुर्विचित्रदीप्तिश्चाय-
> नीयदीप्तिर्वा सोग्निर्विश्वाभिर्गीर्भिः स्तुतिसाधनभूतैः सर्वैर्वाक्यैः क्रियमाणं
> मे मम हवं स्तोत्रं श्रूयाः । शृणोतु । तस्याग्ने रथं श्यावा श्याववर्णावशौ
> वहतः । वा अपि वा रोहिता रोहिद्वर्णावशौ रथं वहतः । रोहिद्वर्णयोरश्वयोः
> श्यावादिवर्णपरिग्रहः स्वेच्छया भवति । विभृत्यो नानास्थानेषु विहृतश्चक्रे । ऋत्विघ्न-
> कारि खलु ॥ करोतेः कर्मणि लिट् ॥

*Gist:* Immortal, all-wise, variously radiant Agni is asked to hear my praise; tawny, red and ruddy horses draw his car; the priests set him in various places.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अमृतः (*amṛtaḥ*)—one free from the nature of death; विचेताः (*vicetāḥ*)—one with distinguished wisdom; चित्रभानुः (*citrabhānuḥ*)—one with wondrous radiance; अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni. विश्वाभिर्गिर्भिः (*viśvābhirgirbhiḥ*)—with all the words composed; मे (*me*)—my; हवं (*havaṃ*)—praise; श्रूयाः (*śrūyāḥ*)—may he hear. श्यावा (*śyāvā*)—those that are brown; वा (*vā*)—or (and); रोहिता (*rohitā*)—those that are red; उत (*uta*)—and; अरुषा (*aruṣā*)—those of saffron colour, being horses. रथं (*rathaṃ*)—they draw the car of Agni (and). विभृतः (*vibhṛtaḥ*)—being carried (by the priests) to various places. चक्रे अह (*cakre aha*)—is he not made?

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May Agni, who is free from the nature of death, of distinguished wisdom and of wondrous radiance, hear this praise composed by me with all kinds of speech. Brown and saffron-coloured horses draw Agni's car. The priests carry such an Agni to various places and establish him there.

**Printed English translation**

May Agni, who is immortal, of manifold wisdom, and wonderful splendour, hear my invocation,(preferred) with all laudations: tawny, or red, or purple horses draw his car; he has been borne in various directions.

### Printed p. 566

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā, Maṇḍala 2, Adhyāya 1, Sūkta 10, p. 566.*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

चित्रभानुः (*citrabhānuḥ*)—विचित्रदीप्तिश्चायनीयदीप्तिर्वा (*vicitradīptiścāyanīyadīptirvā*). Wondrous, that is, of many kinds: one who shines with flames of many colours such as white, red and yellow. The word *bhānu*, formed from the root भा—दीप्तौ (*bhā—dīptau*), is read among the names of day (Ni. 2-20). The word *bhānu* has many meanings: sun, ray, radiance, day and so on. Here the meaning "radiance" is made clear.

श्यावा रथं वहतः रोहिता वा उत अरुषा (*śyāvā rathaṃ vahataḥ rohitā vā uta aruṣā*)—It is said here that the car of Agni is drawn by horses that are श्यावा (*śyāvā*), ash-coloured (the print gives "Tawny"), or रोहिता (*rohitā*), red, or अरुषा (*aruṣā*) (the print gives "purple"), dull red. That Agni's horse is red is stated in the passage

हरी इंद्रस्य । रोहितोऽग्नेः । हरित आदित्यस्य । रासभावश्विनोः । अजाः पूष्णः । पृषत्योर्मरुतां । अरुण्यो गाव उषसः । श्यावाः सवितुः । विश्वरूपा बृहस्पतेः । नियुतो वायोः (*harī iṃdrasya | rohito 'gneḥ | harita ādityasya | rāsabhāvaśvinoḥ | ajāḥ pūṣṇaḥ | pṛṣatyormarutāṃ | aruṇyo gāva uṣasaḥ | śyāvāḥ savituḥ | viśvarūpā bṛhaspateḥ | niyuto vāyoḥ*) (Ni. 2-28).

Yāska has stated the various vehicles of the deities: the two horses called *harī* belong to Indra, the red horse to Agni, the green horse to Āditya, the asses to the Aśvin deities, the goats to Pūṣan, the spotted deer to the Maruts, the red cows to Uṣas, the ash-coloured horses to Savitṛ, the horses of many colours to Bṛhaspati, and the deer called *niyut* to Vāyu. In the present Ṛk, however, it is natural that Agni's horses are red. But the statement that horses of the colours *śyāvā* and *aruṣā* also draw Agni's car may be an exaggeration (उत्पेक्षॆ (*utpekṣè*)). Since Uṣas and Savitṛ rise in the early morning, the sage may have described that the horses *śyāvā* and *aruṣā*, the vehicles of Uṣas and Savitṛ, also draw the car of Agni kindled at such a morning time.

चक्रे विभृत्रः (*cakre vibhṛtraḥ*)—विभृत्रो नानास्थानेषु विहृतश्चक्रे । ऋत्विग्निरकारि खलु (*vibhṛtro nānāsthāneṣu vihṛtaścakre | ṛtvignirakāri khalu*) (as supplied; the bhāṣya on p. 565 reads ऋत्विघ्नकारि (*ṛtvighnakāri*) in the same place). Here it is explained that, since the priests (ऋत्विज (*ṛtvija*)), the Adhvaryu and others, establish Agni in the various places of the sacrifice such as the Āhavanīya, Agni becomes established in many places.

### Printed p. 567

*Running head: Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Aṣṭaka 2, Adhyāya 6, Varga 2, p. 567.*

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒त्ता॒नाया॑मजनय॒न्तु सुषू॑तं॒ भुव॑द॒ग्निः पु॑रु॒षेशा॑सु॒ गर्भः॑ ।
> शिरि॑णायां चिद॒क्तुना॒ महो॑भि॒रप॑रीवृतो वसति॒ प्रचे॑ताः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उत्तानायां । अजनयन् । सुसूतं । भुवत् । अग्निः । पुरुषेशासु । गर्भः ।
> शिरिणायां । चित् । अक्तुना । महोभिः । अपरिवृतः । वसति । प्रचेताः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उत्तानायामुत्तानशयायामरण्यां सुषूतमग्निहोत्रार्थं सुष्टु प्रेरित-
> मग्निमजनयन् । अध्वय्यादय उदपादयन् । सोऽग्निः पुरुषेशासु । पेश इति
> रूपनाम । बहुरूपास्वोषधीषु गर्भो भुवत् । अंतर्वर्तमानतया गर्भ इव
> भवेत् । भवतेर्लिजि रूपं ॥ शिरिणायां । शीर्यंतेऽस्यां भूतानीति शिरणा रात्रिः ।
> तस्यामप्यक्तुना । अंजनादक्तुस्तमः । तेन तमसापरीवृतोऽसंस्पृष्टः आत एव
> महोभिस्तेजोभिर्युक्तः प्रचेताः प्रकृष्टप्रज्ञः सन् वसति । दीप्यमानो वर्तते ॥

*Gist:* The priests produced Agni in the upward-lying araṇi; he becomes the embryo in the many-formed plants and, though not enclosed by night's darkness, dwells shining and wise.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उत्तानायां (*uttānāyāṃ*)—in the araṇi (fire-stick) that lies face upward. सुषूतं (*suṣūtaṃ*)—Agni, urged on (for the Agnihotra). अजनयन् (*ajanayan*)—(the Adhvaryus) caused to be produced. अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—that same Agni. पुरुषेशासु (*puruṣeśāsu*)—in the plants of many forms. गर्भः (*garbhaḥ*)—in the form of an embryo. भुवत् (*bhuvat*)—becomes inside (अंतर्वतिरागुत्तानॆ (*aṃtarvatirāguttānè*) as supplied). शिरिणायां चित् (*śiriṇāyāṃ cit*)—even in the night. आक्तुना (*āktunā*)—by darkness. आपरीवृतः (*āparīvṛtaḥ*)—not enclosed. महोभिः (*mahobhiḥ*)—endowed with his great radiance. प्रचेताः (*pracetāḥ*)—endowed with excellent knowledge. वसति (*vasati*)—remains shining.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The Adhvaryus caused to be produced the Agni who, lying asleep in the araṇi that lies face upward, was urged on for the Agnihotra. That same Agni, in the plants of many forms (text breaks off at the end of this page)

### Printed p. 568

is in the interior, in the form of an embryo. At night, not enveloped by darkness, he continues to shine, endowed with his great radiance and with the knowledge in question.

**Printed English translation**

They have generated(Agni) when soundly sleeping on his upturned (couch): that Agni who is an embryo in many(vegetable) forms, and who, (rendered manifest) by the oblation, abides conscious in the night, unenveloped by darkness.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

उत्तानायां अजनयन् सुसूतं (*uttānāyāṃ ajanayan susūtaṃ*)—*Uttānā* means the lower one of the araṇis (fire-sticks) used for churning out Agni. It is made of the śamī tree. It is broad like a plank, with a slight hollow in the middle. The upper araṇi is made of the wood of the aśvattha tree and is shaped like a stick; it is placed on the lower araṇi of śamī wood and churned, with the help of a rope, as curds are churned. The mouth (or face) of the lower araṇi is always turned upward. Therefore Sāyaṇa explains the word उत्तानायां (*uttānāyāṃ*) here as "in the lower araṇi of śamī wood, which faces upward, of the two araṇis". सुसूतं (*susūtaṃ*) means सुष्टु प्रेरितं (*suṣṭu preritaṃ*), "well urged": that is, the Agni that lay in a state of sleep (slumber) in the araṇis was urged on (awakened) and brought into being by the Adhvaryu and the other priests; this is the chief sense. The Brāhmaṇa sentence शमीगर्भादग्निं मंथति । एषा वा अग्नेर्यज्ञिया तनूः (*śamīgarbhādagniṃ maṃthati | eṣā vā agneryajñiyā tanūḥ*) (Tai. Brā. 1-1-9-1) supports this same sense.

भुवत् अग्निः पुरुषेशासु गर्भः (*bhuvat agniḥ puruṣeśāsu garbhaḥ*)—पेश इति रूपनाम । बहुरूपास्वोषधीषु गर्भो भुवत् । अंतर्वर्तमानतया गर्भ इव भवेत् (*peśa iti rūpanāma | bahurūpāsvoṣadhīṣu garbho bhuvat | aṃtarvartamānatayā garbha iva bhavet*). The word *peśa* is listed among the names of gold (Ni. 2-10) and among the names of form (Ni. 3-13), and there is also an etymology: पेश इति रूपनाम पिंशतेर्विपिशितं भवति (*peśa iti rūpanāma piṃśatervipiśitaṃ bhavati*) (Ni. 8-12). The word *peśa* is derived from the root *piṃś*, which gives the sense of "unfolded, developed", and has the meaning "form". In the present Ṛk, although पुरुषेशासु (*puruṣeśāsu*) would by the sense of its parts mean "in many forms", the commentator here says "in plants of many forms". Since Agni burns plants, trees and the like, it is customary to say that Agni lies hidden in their womb, that is, in their interior (inside). Or, since Agni is produced from the araṇis, which are modifications of trees, one may also explain that Agni dwells in the womb of trees and the like.

### Printed p. 569

शिरिणायां चित् आक्तुना अपरीवृतः (*śiriṇāyāṃ cit āktunā aparīvṛtaḥ*)—The word शिरिणा (*śiriṇā*) is listed among the names of night (Ni. 2-18). This word is derived from the root शीज् स्वप्ने (*śīj svapne*) and is explained as शीर्यंतेऽस्यां भूतानीति शिरिणा रात्रिः (*śīryaṃte 'syāṃ bhūtānīti śiriṇā rātriḥ*) or शाययति प्राणिनः इति शिरिणा (*śāyayati prāṇinaḥ iti śiriṇā*): because living beings sleep at this time, *śiriṇā* means night. Besides this, the word अक्तुः (*aktuḥ*) is also listed among the names of night (Ni. 2-18). This word *aktu* is derived from the root अंजू व्यक्तिम्रक्षणकांतिगतिषु (*aṃjū vyaktimrakṣaṇakāṃtigatiṣu*) and is explained as अज्यते सिच्यतेऽस्यामवश्यायेन जगत् (*ajyate sicyate 'syāmavaśyāyena jagat*): since the whole world is covered by this (darkness), night is called *aktu*. Or, taking the sense of motion, गच्छति वा प्रतिदिनं अक्तुः (*gacchati vā pratidinaṃ aktuḥ*): because it arrives every day, night is called *aktu*. Since in this Ṛk both words शिरिणायां (*śiriṇāyāṃ*) and अक्तुना (*aktunā*) denote night, the commentator explains शिरिणायां (*śiriṇāyāṃ*) as "in the night" and अक्तुना (*aktunā*) as "by darkness or gloom". The sense is: Agni, who is not enveloped, that is, not touched, by the deep darkness of such a night. Since Agni is always shining, he has no contact with the darkness that exists in the night. The chief sense is that such an Agni, endowed with excellent knowledge, shines at night by his own radiance.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> जिघ॑र्म्य॒ग्निं ह॒विषा॑ घृ॒तेन॑ प्रतिक्षि॒यंतं॒ भुव॑नानि॒ विश्वा॑ ।
> पृ॒थुं ति॑र॒श्चा वय॑सा बृ॒हंतं॒ व्यचि॑ष्ठ॒मन्नै॑ रभ॒सं दृशा॑नं ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> जिघर्मि । अग्निं । हविषा । घृतेन । प्रतिक्षियंतं । भुवनानि । विश्वा ।
> पृथुं । तिरश्चा । वयसा । बृहंतं । व्यचिष्ठं । अन्नैः । रभसं । दृश्यानं ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> विश्वा सर्वाणि भुवनानि प्रतिक्षियंतं वैश्वानरात्मनाधितिष्ठंतं पृथुं महांतं
> तिरश्चा सर्वतः प्रवर्तमानेन वयसा पेशसा बृहंतं प्रवृद्धमन्नैर्हविलक्षणैर्व्य-
> चिष्ठं व्याप्तं रभसं बलवंतं दृशानमेवंगुणवत्तया दृश्यमानमुग्निं पुरो-

> *viśvā sarvāṇi bhuvanāni pratikṣiyaṃtaṃ vaiśvānarātmanādhitiṣṭhaṃtaṃ pṛthuṃ mahāṃtaṃ*
> *tiraścā sarvataḥ pravartamānena vayasā peśasā bṛhaṃtaṃ pravṛddhamannairhavilakṣaṇairvya-*
> *ciṣṭhaṃ vyāptaṃ rabhasaṃ balavaṃtaṃ dṛśānamevaṃguṇavattayā dṛśyamānamugniṃ puro-*

### Printed p. 570

> डाशादिलक्षणेन हविषा धृतेन च जिघर्मि ॥ प्रति हैष सर्वाणि भूतानि क्षेति पृथुं
> तिरश्चा वयसा धूमेन बृहंतं व्यचिष्ठमन्नॆ रभसं दृशानमित्यवकाशनवंतमन्नॆर-
> न्नादं दीप्यमानमित्येतदा विश्वत इत्या सर्वतः प्रत्यंचं जुहोमीत्येतदरक्षसेत्य-
> हेळमानेन मनसेदं जुषेतेत्येतन्मर्यश्रीरिति मर्यश्रीरित्येष स्पृहयद्वर्णो
> अग्निर्नाभिमृश इति न हैष मृशेत दीप्यमानो भवतीति ॥

*Gist:* Sāyaṇa: I make Agni blaze with oblation and ghee; he abides in all worlds as Vaiśvānara, vast, nourished by foods, powerful. A Brāhmaṇa passage then explains each word.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all. भुवनानि (*bhuvanāni*)—worlds. प्रतिक्षियंतं (*pratikṣiyaṃtaṃ*)—who pervades in the form of Vaiśvānara. पृथुं (*pṛthuṃ*)—who has greatness. तिरश्चा (*tiraścā*)—moving everywhere. वयसा (*vayasā*)—by form. बृहंतं (*bṛhaṃtaṃ*)—who has grown great. अन्नैः (*annaiḥ*)—by foods in the form of oblations. व्यचिष्ठं (*vyaciṣṭhaṃ*)—who is pervaded. रभसं (*rabhasaṃ*)—who is powerful. दृशानं (*dṛśānaṃ*)—who is seen with all such qualities. अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—Agni. हविसा (*havisā*) (as supplied; the Pada reads हविषा (*haviṣā*))—with the oblation. धृतेन (*dhṛtena*) (as supplied; the Saṃhitā reads घृतेन (*ghṛtena*))—with ghee. जिघर्मि (*jigharmi*)—I cause to shine.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I offer oblation and ghee to Agni, who pervades all the worlds in the form of Vaiśvānara, has greatness, has grown great in a form moving everywhere, is pervaded by the food that is the oblation, and is powerful.

**Printed English translation**

I offer oblations with butter to Agni, who spreads through all regions, vast, expanding, with all-comprehending form, nourished(with sacrificial) food, powerful, conspicuous.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

प्रतिक्षियंतं भुवनानि विश्वा (*pratikṣiyaṃtaṃ bhuvanāni viśvā*)—प्रति ह्येष सर्वाणि भूतानि क्षेति (*prati hyeṣa sarvāṇi bhūtāni kṣeti*). This Agni dwells in all living beings in the form of Vaiśvānara. That is, as Vaiśvānara fire he dwells in herbs, forest trees, creepers, trees and the like; as the digestive fire (*jāṭharāgni*) in the classes of beings such as men; as terrestrial fire in the earth; as the fire of lightning (flash) in the atmosphere; and as the sun in heaven.
